- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.22429v1
- Title: Dilaton-Axion Wormhole Partition Function Away from the BPS Endpoint
- Authors: Soo-Jong Rey
- Categories: hep-th (primary); hep-th; gr-qc; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22429v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22429v1.pdf

Abstract:
I determine the leading finite-radius correction to the two-boundary response of a charged axion wormhole near its BPS endpoint. With $δ=a_*^2$, exact radial matching in a product axion--dilaton/saxion system gives a boundary eigenvalue that vanishes as $O(δ)$ and an off-diagonal Hessian entry of order $O(δ^2)$. Their combination produces a cross-boundary covariance linear in $δ$, with a negative first sub-leading correction on the branch studied here. I determine the semiclassical range in which the quadratic approximation remains controlled and identify the first finite-radius angular resonance, $δ^2\log(δ/δ_0)$. Under a uniform rescaling of all active charges, the coefficient of the linear cross-boundary response scales as the inverse square of the charge scale. I then average the inverse response over the chiral Gaussian ensemble describing the BPS endpoint. Boundary reduction preserves the hard-edge index. Multiplication by the matched BPS sector weight converts the normalized response into the leading finite-radius two-source coefficient.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.22501v1
- Title: Entropy Production and Thermalization in Heavy-Quark Langevin Dynamics
- Authors: A. M. Silva
- Categories: hep-ph (primary); hep-ph; cond-mat.stat-mech; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22501v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22501v1.pdf

Abstract:
Heavy quarks are often treated as Brownian probes of the quark-gluon plasma. We formulate the irreversible entropy balance of their momentum distribution and separate the production generated by collisions from the driving caused by an evolving medium. For linear dynamics the production splits into two positive contributions, from directed momentum and from the momentum width. A static bath obeys an exact H-theorem. A cooling bath adds an explicit driving term, and longitudinal Bjorken expansion enters as a separate kinematic drive rather than as collisional production. For a Bjorken temperature protocol we derive closed-form strong- and weak-coupling limits of the total production. At strong coupling it exceeds the initial relative entropy by a term linear in the relaxation time, whose quasistatic part has the thermodynamic-length form. At weak coupling it vanishes inversely with the relaxation time. A positive strong-coupling slope therefore guarantees maximal dissipation at intermediate coupling. The same structure holds for a relativistic Fokker-Planck equation with a Jüttner stationary state, solved numerically for charm and bottom quarks under the temperature protocol. Linear-response coefficients computed from the static generator reproduce both limits, and the numerical entropy balance is satisfied to high accuracy.

- Title: Trapping of nanofabricated SiO$_2$ high-aspect-ratio discs in an optical lattice
- Authors: Shelby Klomp, Shafaq Gulzar Elahi, Scott Grudichak, Chethn Galla, Zhiyuan Wang, Mark Nguyen, George Winstone, Katarina Boskovic Guy, et al.
- Categories: physics.optics (primary); physics.optics; astro-ph.IM; gr-qc; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22614v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22614v1.pdf

Abstract:
We describe the nanofabrication and loading of lithographically patterned, high aspect ratio $10$ $μ$m diameter silicon dioxide discs with thickness of $400$ nm into an optical standing wave trap in vacuum. We obtain motional spectra of the center of mass motion of the discs using a combination of interferometric and imaging methods. The method is capable of high-efficiency loading and optical trapping using small numbers of objects, of the order of a few particles, with customized geometries and sizes compatible with wafer scale nanofabrication methods. Disc-like particles in optical traps have been identified as promising candidates for detectors of high frequency gravitational waves or as sensitive accelerometers.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.22639v1
- Title: Universal Threshold Laws for Dipolar Evaporation of Molecular Fermi Gases
- Authors: Kevin B. Xu, John L. Bohn, Reuben R. W. Wang
- Categories: cond-mat.quant-gas (primary); cond-mat.quant-gas; physics.atom-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22639v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22639v1.pdf

Abstract:
Fermi statistics suppress the elastic collisions that rethermalize a Fermi gas during evaporative cooling, creating a fundamental low-temperature bottleneck for producing quantum degenerate gases. We show that, for shielded single-component ultracold dipolar molecular Fermi gases in the Wigner threshold regime, this bottleneck is governed by universal threshold laws fixed by the effective dimensionality of the gas. In this regime, Fermi suppression of rethermalization scales quadratically with temperature in 3D but only linearly in 2D. Consequently, the thermalization-to-loss ratio is more favorable in 2D than in 3D, and these results suggest that 2D geometries are better suited for reaching deep degeneracy. We also present ab initio close-coupling calculations that agree well with experimental measurements on Fermi degenerate gases of KRb molecules, supporting our conclusions.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.22710v1
- Title: Area Theorems for Quantum Error Correction
- Authors: Avijit Mandal, Mert Gökduman, Henry D. Pfister
- Categories: cs.IT (primary); cs.IT; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22710v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22710v1.pdf

Abstract:
The extrinsic information transfer (EXIT) and generalized EXIT (GEXIT) area theorems provide fundamental conservation laws that connect the rate of classical codes to the performance of maximum-a-posteriori decoding on binary memoryless channels. In this work, we generalize these area theorems to quantum error correction. We first develop a coset-entropy formalism for nested linear codes, where the message specifies a coset and encoder randomization selects a codeword within that coset. The area under the resulting coset (G)EXIT function equals the normalized dimension of the quotient. For stabilizer codes, the measured syndrome restricts the Pauli error but does not always determine its logical effect, and the remaining uncertainty is an entropy over logical correction classes. We show that this entropy equals a classical coset entropy associated with the stabilizer group and the Pauli operators that commute with it. The resulting quantum GEXIT function has area equal to the number of encoded qubits per channel use for any stabilizer code, and this applies to the depolarizing channel through its joint four-valued Pauli-error distribution. For CSS codes on the quantum erasure channel and under independent bit- and phase-flip noise, the logical correction problem separates into two binary coset-decoding problems. On the quantum erasure channel, the duality between the stabilizer group and the Pauli operators that commute with it gives a folding identity at complementary erasure probabilities. Using this and the area theorem, we derive an upper bound on the rate of code families with vanishing logical error probability that matches the capacity of the quantum erasure channel. For the depolarizing channel, we also derive a rate-dependent converse, using a connection between correction-class entropy and coherent information to establish an inequality involving the complementary channel.

- Title: Development of a fabrication-to-benchtop process for SiN-based quantum devices
- Authors: Connor Kupchak, Abubaker Tareki, Tara Moradi, Patrick Laferriere, Niall Tait, Khaled Mnaymneh
- Categories: physics.optics (primary); physics.optics; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22776v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22776v1.pdf

Abstract:
Silicon nitride has emerged as a key material for the advancement of future quantum communication and computational devices. However, challenges persist in establishing a comprehensive development process, spanning from fabrication to optical characterization, essential for advancing these devices to their next stage of potential. Maintaining control over the entire process, from start to finish, alongside device modeling, enables more efficient and timely iteration and optimization. Here we introduce a fabrication-to-benchtop process. We employ an in-house stoichiometric recipe for device fabrication and conduct comprehensive characterization of optical losses and material properties. Understanding and controlling the refractive index of the device are vital for predicting device geometries and group indices. Implementing the refractive indices of our fabricated devices, we simulate microring resonators and demonstrate their application in generating optical solitons.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.22867v1
- Title: Chirality-resolved imaging of spin waves with a quantum spin
- Authors: Enbo Zhang, Tiancheng Zheng, Bo-Yuan Yang, Shreyes Nallan, Chengchao Xu, Mark Ku, Jian-Gang Zhu
- Categories: cond-mat.mes-hall (primary); cond-mat.mes-hall; physics.app-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22867v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22867v1.pdf

Abstract:
Spin waves carry their propagation direction in the handedness of their circularly polarized stray field, a property inaccessible to scalar magnetometers. In the Damon-Eshbach surface wave of an in-plane magnetized film, the dipolar field above the film rotates with a handedness fixed by the direction of travel. The nitrogen-vacancy (NV) centre in diamond is an intrinsic polarization analyser: a right-handed field about the NV axis drives one spin transition, and a left-handed field drives the other, in both the ground and the excited state. Here, we exploit the two excited-state transitions, which lie inside the spin-wave band of yttrium iron garnet (YIG), as a pair of chirality channels and image Damon-Eshbach waves in both directions. Because reflection from an etched boundary reverses the handedness, the incident and reflected waves appear as two lines in one spectrum and are imaged separately. In the ground state, the same rule applies, and the selected field drives Rabi oscillations, a route to chirality-selective control of the NV spin by magnons. Reading the two channels gives the direction of a spin wave at one point, without a phase reference. This advances chirality-resolved quantum sensing and provides insight into chiral spin-wave dynamics.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.23105v1
- Title: Nonergodicity from Hilbert-Subspace Cloning
- Authors: Yuuya Chiba, Ryusuke Hamazaki
- Categories: cond-mat.stat-mech (primary); cond-mat.stat-mech; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23105v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.23105v1.pdf

Abstract:
We identify a class of nonintegrable quantum many-body systems composed of single Pauli strings that possess an extensive number of noncommuting local conserved quantities and exhibit partial ergodicity breaking due to exponentially degenerate energy spectra. We show that such systems exhibit a novel mechanism, Hilbert-subspace cloning, where the identical chaotic Hamiltonian acts on each of exponentially many disconnected Hilbert subspaces. Although the dynamics within each clone thermalizes, quantum coherence between clones remains protected and detectable by local observables. Consequently, the long-time behavior of local observables is described by a coherence-preserving partial Gibbs ensemble, where the hidden subsystem retains its initial quantum coherence.

- Title: From quantum emitters to technological platforms: a perspective on semiconductor quantum dots
- Authors: Klaus D. Jöns, Dorian A. Gangloff, Doris E. Reiter
- Categories: cond-mat.mes-hall (primary); cond-mat.mes-hall; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23106v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.23106v1.pdf

Abstract:
Photonic quantum technologies are based on controlled photon states. To generate these, quantum dots have emerged as excellent sources of single and entangled photons. In this Perspective, we briefly review the chronological development of quantum dots as photon sources, from their beginnings as quantum emitters in the early 2000s to the advanced photonic devices being developed today. We highlight the interplay between fundamental research and industrial aspects before discussing the photonic quantum technologies we expect to emerge in the near future.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.23108v1
- Title: Multiscale Quantum-Classical Simulation of Protein Autoproteolysis in a Million-Atom Membrane Environment
- Authors: Alexander Orlov, Alexis Ralli, Tim Weaving, Michael Williams de la Bastida, Reuben Martin, Zohim Chandani, Minh Chung, P. V. Sriluckshmy, et al.
- Categories: q-bio.BM (primary); q-bio.BM; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23108v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.23108v1.pdf

Abstract:
Predicting chemical reactions in biomolecules is challenging because reactive centers are embedded within complex environments that strongly influence their energetics and mechanisms. Here, we present a hybrid quantum-classical workflow that combines molecular dynamics, density functional theory, and correlated wavefunction methods, tightly integrating CPUs, GPUs, and quantum processing units (QPUs) to enable simulations across multiple length and accuracy scales. We apply this approach to ADGRG1 autoproteolysis, a maturation reaction in an adhesion G protein-coupled receptor that has not previously been studied using correlated electronic-structure methods. Our simulations treat a biologically realistic system containing more than one million atoms, including the full-length uncleaved receptor embedded in an endoplasmic-reticulum membrane, while incorporating a 54-qubit IQM quantum processor and up to 1,200 NVIDIA H100 GPUs. These results demonstrate how quantum computers can serve as accelerators within scalable scientific workflows, extending correlated electronic-structure methods to biologically relevant systems.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.23268v1
- Title: Unconditional Quantum PAC Learning Advantage in the Large Error Regime
- Authors: Igor C. Oliveira
- Categories: cs.CC (primary); cs.CC; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23268v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.23268v1.pdf

Abstract:
We establish an unconditional super-polynomial separation between quantum and classical PAC learning of Boolean functions under the uniform distribution in the large error regime. For every advantage $λ(n) = n^{-ω(1)}$, we construct a concept class that a polynomial-time quantum learner learns to agreement $1/2+λ(n)$ with success probability at least $1-1/n^k$. The learner uses only quantum random examples and outputs a polynomial-size deterministic classical circuit. In contrast, no polynomial-time classical learner achieves agreement $1/2 + λ(n)/n^k$ with success probability greater than $1/n^k$, even with membership queries.   To achieve this, we develop a nearly-optimal quantum learner that uses quantum random examples to produce a hypothesis achieving a prescribed correlation with an arbitrary Boolean function. The learner generalizes Fourier sampling from parity hypotheses to quadratic hypotheses. While standard Fourier sampling is insensitive to the sign of the correlation, we use interference to bias sampling toward positively correlated hypotheses. This is combined with a careful analysis of the resulting distribution to boost both the learner's success probability and its agreement with the target function. We believe that the resulting quantum learning algorithm might be of independent interest.   Finally, although a robust quantum PAC learning advantage holds unconditionally in the large error regime, we note that an analogous separation between quantum and classical learning in the small error regime would imply that $\mathsf{NP} \nsubseteq \mathsf{BPP}$.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.23573v1
- Title: Action-Quantized Scar Multiplets and Dissipative Cat States from Fractal Period-Locking in the Open Quantum Baker Map
- Authors: Leonardo Ermann
- Categories: nlin.CD (primary); nlin.CD; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23573v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.23573v1.pdf

Abstract:
We open the quantum baker map with a position window of width $w$ and center $q_c$. Classical pruning tiles the $(q_c,w)$ plane into diamonds where the $T$ leading resonances form root-of-unity multiplets. At $w=1/2$ one periodic orbit survives for almost every $q_c$, and the multiplets are quantized without free parameters: their modulus $2^{-1/2}$ saturates the pressure bound and their phase is the orbit action. They form cycling multicomponent cat states, and the opening becomes one mid-circuit measurement, enabling spectroscopy with $\sim10^2$ shots on quantum processors.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.23580v1
- Title: Holographic two-point functions across a PT symmetry-breaking transition
- Authors: Zuo Wang, Liang He
- Categories: hep-th (primary); hep-th; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23580v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.23580v1.pdf

Abstract:
In parity-time (PT)-symmetric non-Hermitian systems, an exceptional point, where eigenvalues and eigenvectors coalesce, typically separates a real spectrum from complex-conjugate eigenvalues. In logarithmic conformal field theories (LCFTs), such an exceptional point appears in the spectrum of scaling dimensions. Holographic models of LCFTs, such as the dipole model, are built exactly at this point. Here, we deform the holographic dipole model so that its two coalescing scaling dimensions split into either two real ones or a complex-conjugate pair. We obtain the renormalized two-point functions in closed form. They change continuously from a linear combination of two power laws, through the logarithmic form of the LCFT at the exceptional point, to log-periodic oscillations. For any pair of scalar primary operators with real mixing under dilatations, the conformal Ward identities fix this form up to two normalization constants, which the holographic dictionary determines. In PT-symmetric theories with such a dilatation operator, real and complex-conjugate scaling dimensions correspond to the PT-unbroken and PT-broken phases. In the BTZ black hole at finite temperature, the difference between the quasinormal frequencies associated with the two scaling dimensions changes from purely imaginary to purely real across the real-to-complex spectral transition. Correspondingly, the linear response changes from overdamped to underdamped, with critical damping at the exceptional point.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.23681v1
- Title: Impact of Irrep Truncations on Quantum Simulations of QCD
- Authors: Patrick Draper, Drishti Gupta
- Categories: hep-lat (primary); hep-lat; hep-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23681v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.23681v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum simulations of lattice gauge theories with continuous gauge groups require a choice of basis (how states of the gauge theory map to the qubit computational basis) and truncation scheme (which of the infinitely many gauge theory basis states should be omitted in the map to the finite dimensional qubit Hilbert space.) The most common choices are adapted to an electric basis for the gauge theory, where the basis states for each link are associated with irreducible representations of the gauge group. Electric bases and truncations are generally advantageous for preserving gauge symmetries and simulating the strong coupling regime, but their effectiveness in the weak coupling regimes of physical interest are less well understood. In this work, we compute relative probabilities $p(R')/p(R)$ for finding a link in the irreps $R,R'$ in the ground state of SU(3) lattice gauge theory using Euclidean Monte Carlo methods and weak-coupling lattice perturbation theory. The observables that compute these relative probabilities are constructed by introducing seams into an anisotropic lattice, and by a similar construction we are also able to measure relative probabilities of different site singlets (ways that electric flux braids together into a gauge singlet) at each vertex. With these methods we can quantify, for example, the importance of higher electric flux states in the weak coupling ground state. These results directly inform what truncations are needed to accurately capture non-abelian gauge dynamics near the continuum limit.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.23770v1
- Title: Scaling of entanglement with distance between subsystems: detection of phase transitions in the $t'$-$π$-flux model
- Authors: Maksim Ulybyshev, Gergő Roósz, Gabriele Bellomia, Karsten Held, Fakher Assaad, Frederic Bippus
- Categories: cond-mat.str-el (primary); cond-mat.str-el; cond-mat.stat-mech; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23770v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.23770v1.pdf

Abstract:
The system-size scaling of bipartite entanglement can be used to detect phase transitions. We develop a related approach for strongly correlated electron models where large density matrices or large system size scaling are inaccessible. To this end, we demonstrate that phase transitions of various kinds are signaled by a sharp signature in the first- or second-order logarithmic derivatives of the negativity measuring entanglement between small clusters at varying distances. Crucially, we show how to mitigate the effect of quantum Monte Carlo noise at large distances when computing the negativity. Our focus is on the $t'$-$π$-flux Hubbard model using auxiliary-field quantum Monte Carlo simulations. It has the particular advantage of showing three distinct phase transitions in a single model: semimetal to band insulator, semimetal to antiferromagnetic Mott insulator, and a magnetic order-disorder transition belonging to the Gaussian, Gross-Neveu, and O(3) Wilson-Fisher universality classes, respectively.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.23781v1
- Title: Transport and thermalization under noncommuting localized constraints
- Authors: Amir Nesturov, Attila Takács, Lenart Zadnik
- Categories: cond-mat.stat-mech (primary); cond-mat.stat-mech; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23781v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.23781v1.pdf

Abstract:
We investigate the effects of spatially localized conserved quantities on transport and thermalization in quantum and classical many-body systems. We consider several related models: the integrable quantum Volterra model, its nonintegrable classical limit, and two cellular automata derived from the latter. Although localized constraints are often associated with lattice gauge theories, our models do not admit such an interpretation. In systems with discrete degrees of freedom, these constraints induce uniform fragmentation of the Hilbert or configuration space. In the quantum Volterra model, we derive an exact mapping of the dynamics within each Hilbert-space fragment onto a quantum chiral Potts chain. We find that energy transport is ballistic, with identical transport coefficients across all fragments. In the nonintegrable classical limit, energy transport is diffusive, and we similarly find the diffusion constant to be independent of whether or not the constraints are fixed (resolved). Finally, in the cellular automata, we show that, despite leaving transport unaffected, localized constraints lead to a strong breakdown of local ergodicity.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.23810v1
- Title: Experimental Demonstration of a Balanced Travelling Wave Parametric Amplifier
- Authors: Nikita Klimovich, Peter K. Day, Henry G. Leduc, Boon-Kok Tan
- Categories: physics.ins-det (primary); physics.ins-det; cond-mat.supr-con; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23810v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.23810v1.pdf

Abstract:
Superconducting travelling-wave parametric amplifiers (TWPAs) provide broadband gain, low added noise, and high dynamic range, making them attractive for applications requiring wide instantaneous bandwidth. This is crucial for amplifying multiplexed narrow band signals spread over a wide frequency range, such as qubit resonator arrays, or amplifying inherently wide band signals, such as in spectrometers or radiometers. In many cases, however, the useful bandwidth of a TWPA is limited by the production of the idler tone at an image frequency. For three-wave-mixing (3WM) TWPAs, for example, an input signal at $f_s$ is indistinguishable from one at $f_p - f_s$, which can produce spectral ambiguity when the input spectrum is broadband or not known in advance. We experimentally demonstrate a balanced architecture that doubles the instantaneous image-rejected bandwidth available to a 3WM TWPA by spatially separating the amplified signal from the output pump and generated idler, which resolves the sideband degeneracy. The architecture consists of two amplifiers connected between a pair of 180$^{\circ}$ hybrid couplers, with the signal and pump injected and output through the sum and difference ports, respectively. Using two kinetic-inductance TWPAs, we demonstrate approximately 20 dB of signal gain across nearly an octave of bandwidth, together with pump rejection below --20 dB and an average image rejection of approximately --15 dB over the operating band. These results establish balanced TWPAs as a practical approach to broadband parametric amplification, enabling improved contamination free bandwidth and eliminating the need for complex filtering.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.23815v1
- Title: Anyonic Neural Network Wavefunctions
- Authors: Andres Perez Fadon, Pok Man Tam, Leyna Shackleton
- Categories: cond-mat.str-el (primary); cond-mat.str-el; cond-mat.mes-hall; cond-mat.quant-gas; hep-th; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23815v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.23815v1.pdf

Abstract:
The macroscopic behavior of a non-interacting gas of Abelian anyons has remained unsettled for many decades. Prior numerical methods employing exact diagonalization in the continuum and on the plane have been limited to small system sizes, at most $N = 4$ anyons. Using the rapidly developing technology of neural network wavefunctions, we develop variational ansatze appropriate for studying continuum models of the Abelian anyon gas. The neural network architecture, which we call "AnyNet", is capable of exactly enforcing the non-analytic short-distance behavior induced by the anyonic statistics. We demonstrate excellent agreement with exact diagonalization for $N=4$ anyons, obtain accurate results up to $N = 16$ for intermediate statistics, and study up to $N=20$ for almost-fermionic anyons, matching perturbative results within a 0.4% discrepancy. We use this methodology to study anyons in a harmonic trap, and anyonic atoms as a model for anyon binding to impurities. We study the structure of anyonic wavefunctions as the statistics parameter is varied. We focus on the single-particle density, the pair density, the angular momentum, and the scaling of the wavefunction as multiple anyons coalesce. This work opens doors to understanding the macroscopic behavior of anyons, for which rich phases have been predicted theoretically.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2001.04383v4
- Title: MIP*=RE
- Authors: Zhengfeng Ji, Anand Natarajan, Thomas Vidick, John Wright, Henry Yuen
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.CC; math.OA
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2001.04383v4  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2001.04383v4.pdf

Abstract:
We show that the class MIP* of languages that can be decided by a classical verifier interacting with multiple all-powerful quantum provers sharing entanglement is equal to the class RE of recursively enumerable languages. Our proof builds upon the quantum low-degree test of (Natarajan and Vidick, FOCS 2018) and the classical low-individual degree test of (Ji, et al., 2020) by integrating recent developments from (Natarajan and Wright, FOCS 2019) and combining them with the recursive compression framework of (Fitzsimons et al., STOC 2019).   An immediate byproduct of our result is that there is an efficient reduction from the Halting Problem to the problem of deciding whether a two-player nonlocal game has entangled value $1$ or at most $1/2$. Using a known connection, undecidability of the entangled value implies a negative answer to Tsirelson's problem: we show, by providing an explicit example, that the closure $C_{qa}$ of the set of quantum tensor product correlations is strictly included in the set $C_{qc}$ of quantum commuting correlations. Following work of (Fritz, Rev. Math. Phys. 2012) and (Junge et al., J. Math. Phys. 2011) our results provide a refutation of Connes' embedding conjecture from the theory of von Neumann algebras.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2108.12209v5
- Title: Exponential clustering of bipartite quantum entanglement at arbitrary temperatures
- Authors: Tomotaka Kuwahara, Keiji Saito
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.dis-nn; cond-mat.quant-gas; cond-mat.stat-mech; math-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.12209v5  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2108.12209v5.pdf

Abstract:
Macroscopic quantum effects play central roles in the appearance of inexplicable phenomena in low-temperature quantum many-body physics. Such macroscopic quantumness is often evaluated using long-range entanglement, i.e., entanglement in the macroscopic length scale. The long-range entanglement not only characterizes the novel quantum phases but also serves as a critical resource for quantum computation. Thus, the problem that arises is under which conditions can the long-range entanglement be stable even at room temperatures. Here, we show that bi-partite long-range entanglement is unstable at arbitrary temperatures and exponentially decays with distance. Our theorem provides a no-go theorem on the existence of the long-range entanglement. The obtained results are consistent with the existing observations that long-range entanglement at non-zero temperatures can exist in topologically ordered phases, where tripartite correlations are dominant. In the derivation of our result, we introduce a quantum correlation defined by the convex roof of the standard correlation function. We establish an exponential clustering theorem for generic quantum many-body systems for such a quantum correlation at arbitrary temperatures, which yields our main result by relating quantum correlation to quantum entanglement. As a simple application of our analytical techniques, we derived a general limit on the Wigner-Yanase-Dyson skew information and the quantum Fisher information, which will attract significant attention in the field of quantum metrology. Our work reveals novel general aspects of low-temperature quantum physics and sheds light on the characterization of long-range entanglement.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2404.03268v4
- Title: Molecular Ground State Simulation by Subspace Restriction and Hund's Rule
- Authors: Tsung-Chi Chiang, Calvin Ku, Jyh-Pin Chou, Alice Hu, Peng-Jen Chen, Ching-Jui Lai
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03268v4  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.03268v4.pdf

Abstract:
The Subspace Restriction Scheme (SRS) is presented to address the qubit and resource constraints of molecular simulation on near-term quantum hardware. SRS restricts a molecular Hamiltonian to a selected determinant subspace before qubit encoding, so a $D$-dimensional subspace requires only $q=\lceil\log_2D\rceil$ qubits. We apply SRS to the established seniority-resolved Multi-Hund Subspaces $\mathrm{MHS}(Ω)$ and develop compact encodings for these sectors. At fixed seniority and filling fraction, MHS asymptotically uses about half as many qubits as a compact particle-conserving encoding. For the $\mathrm{H}_{22}$ chain, the register shrinks from $44$ to $20$ qubits. Compression can nevertheless increase Pauli-operator density and does not always reduce measurement cost. In noiseless VQE benchmarks, shallow circuits reach within $1.6$~mHa of the exact subspace minima using only $4$--$30$ CNOT gates. However, the corresponding subspace minima for LiH, HF, OH, and CH remain $4.39$--$9.73$~mHa above FCI. Full-space UCCSD reaches chemical accuracy but requires hundreds to thousands of CNOT gates. SRS therefore provides a controlled resource--accuracy trade-off when register width is the binding constraint and the target state is well represented within a selected seniority sector.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2407.07609v2
- Title: Continuous variable dense coding under realistic non-ideal scenarios
- Authors: Mrinmoy Samanta, Ayan Patra, Rivu Gupta, Aditi Sen De
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07609v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2407.07609v2.pdf

Abstract:
We analyze the continuous variable (CV) dense coding protocol between a single sender and a single receiver when affected by noise in the shared and encoded states, as well as when the decoding is imperfect. We present a formalism to derive the dense coding capacity (DCC) of generic two-mode Gaussian states when affected by Gaussian noise models. When the constituent modes are affected by quantum-limited amplifiers, pure-loss channels, and environmental interactions, together with an inefficient decoding mechanism comprising imperfect double-homodyne detection, we specifically investigate the performance of the two-mode squeezed vacuum state (TMSV) by varying the strength of the noise. Through numerical simulations, we further show that the negative conditional entropy may be used to assess the quantum advantage of CV dense coding in Gaussian setups. While comparing the TMSV with other Gaussian states, we identify a class of pure Gaussian states capable of furnishing a capacity equal to that of the TMSV, under equal energy constraints in the ideal regime. Further, we demonstrate that, while the TMSV state is more susceptible to noise, it still provides the maximum quantum advantage in the DC protocol under practical conditions.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2412.09598v2
- Title: Bottlenecks in quantum channels and finite temperature phases of matter
- Authors: Tibor Rakovszky, Benedikt Placke, Nikolas P. Breuckmann, Vedika Khemani
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.stat-mech
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.09598v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.09598v2.pdf

Abstract:
We prove an analog of the "bottleneck theorem," well-known for classical Markov chains, for Markovian quantum channels. In particular, we show that if two regions (subspaces) of Hilbert space are separated by a region that has very low weight in the channel's steady state, then states initialized on one side of this barrier will take a long time to relax, putting a lower bound on the mixing time in terms of an appropriately defined "quantum bottleneck ratio." Importantly, this bottleneck ratio involves not only the probabilities of the relevant subspaces, but also the size of off-diagonal matrix elements between them. For low temperature quantum many-body systems, we use the bottleneck theorem to bound the performance of any quasilocal Gibbs sampler. This leads to a new perspective on thermally stable quantum phases in terms of a decomposition of the Gibbs state into multiple components separated by bottlenecks. As a concrete application, we show rigorously that weakly perturbed commuting projector models with extensive energy barriers (which include certain classical and quantum expander codes) have exponentially large mixing times

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2503.11613v3
- Title: Floquet-ADAPT-VQE: A Quantum Algorithm to Simulate Non-Equilibrium Physics in Periodically Driven Systems
- Authors: Abhishek Kumar, Karunya Shirali, Nicholas J. Mayhall, Sophia E. Economou, Edwin Barnes
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11613v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.11613v3.pdf

Abstract:
Periodically driven quantum systems exhibit many fascinating phenomena absent in equilibrium systems, but their simulation is more challenging than that of static systems. Consequently, quantum simulation of these systems offers greater opportunity for achieving quantum advantage. To build the foundation for simulating time-periodic Hamiltonians, we utilize the Floquet-Hilbert space formalism to transform the Hamiltonian into a time-independent form and provide its Pauli decomposition. Within this formalism, we develop a hybrid quantum-classical algorithm, Floquet-ADAPT-VQE, that produces three key quantities that govern periodically driven quantum dynamics: the quasienergy, the associated Floquet eigenstates, and expectation values of observables with respect to these eigenstates. The latter is achieved by applying a fixed-depth quantum circuit to an initial Floquet eigenstate---an efficient construction made possible by the Floquet-Hilbert space formalism. We also identify a suitable auxiliary initial state whose squared Floquet energy is independent of the number of auxiliary qubits as well as the driving frequency, which leads to better convergence with fewer ADAPT iterations. We demonstrate our algorithm by performing numerical simulations on a periodically driven XYZ model with a magnetic field. We also explore potential applications of our algorithm for studying various non-equilibrium phenomena in periodically driven systems.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2504.01073v3
- Title: Predicting Dynamics from Flows of the Eigenstate Thermalization Hypothesis
- Authors: Dominik Hahn, David M. Long, Marin Bukov, Anushya Chandran
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01073v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.01073v3.pdf

Abstract:
Analytical treatments of far-from-equilibrium quantum dynamics are few, even in well-thermalizing systems. The celebrated eigenstate thermalization hypothesis (ETH) provides a post hoc ansatz for the matrix elements of observables in the eigenbasis of a thermalizing Hamiltonian, given various response functions of those observables as input. However, the ETH cannot predict these response functions. We introduce a procedure, dubbed the statistical Jacobi approximation (SJA), to update the ETH ansatz after a perturbation to the Hamiltonian and predict perturbed response functions. The Jacobi algorithm diagonalizes the perturbation through a sequence of two-level rotations. The SJA implements these rotations statistically assuming the ETH throughout the diagonalization procedure, and generates integrodifferential flow equations for various form factors in the ETH ansatz. We approximately solve these flow equations using statistical information about Jacobi rotations as input, and predict both quench dynamics and autocorrelators in the thermal state of the perturbed Hamiltonian. The predicted dynamics agree with exact numerics in both random matrix models and spin chains, outperforming second-order time-dependent perturbation theory.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2508.21419v2
- Title: Characterization of Linear Measurements in Cavity Optomechanics: Examples and Applications
- Authors: F. Bemani, O. Černotík, R. Filip
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21419v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.21419v2.pdf

Abstract:
Detailed understanding of physical measurements is essential for devising efficient metrological strategies and measurement-feedback schemes, as well as finding fundamental limitations on measurement sensitivity. In the quantum regime, measurements modify the state of the system of interest through measurement backaction as a direct consequence of the Heisenberg principle. In cavity optomechanics and electromechanics, a plethora of strategies exist for measuring the mechanical motion using electromagnetic fields, each leading to different competition between measurement imprecision and backaction. While this range of techniques allows broad applications of optomechanical and electromechanical devices, it makes direct comparison of different measurement methods difficult. We develop a formalism for quantifying the performance of optomechanical measurements using a few relevant figures of merit. Our approach is inspired by similar characterizations in quantum optics and quantifies the main properties of quantum measurements -- the ability to distinguish different quantum states and preservation of signal in the presence of measurement noise. We demonstrate our concept on the most common optomechanical measurements -- displacement detection, coherent quantum noise cancellation, and quantum nondemolition measurements -- and perform detailed analysis of errors in optomechanical nondemolition measurements. This newly acquired knowledge allows us to propose a strategy for quantum nondemolition measurements in levitodynamics using coherent scattering. Our results complement existing knowledge of linear optomechanical interactions and open the way to new understanding of optomechanical measurements, thus allowing also novel applications of optomechanical devices in fundamental physics and quantum technologies.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2509.15204v3
- Title: Circuit-based characterization of finite-temperature quantum phases and self-correcting quantum memory
- Authors: Ruochen Ma, Vedika Khemani, Shengqi Sang
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.stat-mech
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15204v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.15204v3.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum phases at zero temperature can be characterized as equivalence classes under local unitary transformations: two ground states within a gapped phase can be transformed into each other via a local unitary circuit. We generalize this circuit-based characterization of phases to systems at finite-temperature thermal equilibrium described by Gibbs states. We construct a channel circuit that approximately transforms one Gibbs state into another provided the two are connected by a path in parameter space along which a certain correlation-decay condition holds. For finite-dimensional systems of linear size $L$ and approximation error $ε$, the locality of the circuit is ${\rm polylog}({\rm poly}(L)/ε)$. The correlation-decay condition, which we specify, is expected to be satisfied in the interior of many noncritical thermal phases, including those displaying discrete symmetry breaking and topological order. As an application, we show that any system in the same thermal phase as a zero-temperature topological code coherently preserves quantum information for a macroscopically long time, establishing self-correction as a universal property of thermal phases. As part of the proof, we provide explicit encoding and decoding channel circuits to encode information into, and decode it from, a system in thermal equilibrium.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2510.13332v2
- Title: Hybrid boson sampling-neural network architecture for enhanced classification
- Authors: Mohammad Sharifian, Abolfazl Bayat
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.13332v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.13332v2.pdf

Abstract:
Demonstration of quantum advantage for classical machine learning tasks remains a central goal for quantum technologies and artificial intelligence. Two major bottlenecks to this goal are the high dimensionality of practical datasets and the limited performance of near-term quantum computers. Boson sampling is among the few models for which experiments have claimed quantum advantage, yet it has limited practical applications. Here, we propose a hybrid framework that combines the computational power of boson sampling with the adaptability of neural networks to construct quantum kernels that enhance support vector machine classification. The neural network adapts the data features onto a programmable boson sampling circuit, producing quantum states that span a high-dimensional Hilbert space and enable improved classification performance. Using four datasets with various classes, we demonstrate that our model outperforms classical linear and sigmoid kernels. These results highlight the potential of boson sampling-based quantum kernels for practical quantum-enhanced machine learning.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2511.07617v3
- Title: The three kinds of three-qubit entanglement
- Authors: Szilárd Szalay
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; math-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.07617v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.07617v3.pdf

Abstract:
We construct an important missing piece in the entanglement theory of pure three-qubit states, which is a polynomial measure of W-entanglement. This works in parallel to the three-tangle, which is a polynomial measure of GHZ-entanglement, and to the bipartite concurrence, which is a polynomial measure of bipartite entanglement. We also show that these entanglement measures are ordered, the bipartite measure is larger than the W measure, which is larger than the GHZ measure. It is meaningful then to consider these three types of three-qubit entanglement, which are also ordered, bipartite is weaker than W, which is weaker than GHZ, in parallel to the order of the three kinds of equivalence classes of entangled three-qubit states.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2512.20793v2
- Title: Higher-Dimensional Information Lattice: Quantum State Characterization through Inclusion-Exclusion Local Information
- Authors: Ian Matthias Flór, Claudia Artiaco, Thomas Klein Kvorning, Jens H. Bardarson
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.mes-hall; cond-mat.other
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.20793v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.20793v2.pdf

Abstract:
Developing a local description of quantum information is a central problem in quantum many-body physics: the goal is to identify how correlations are organized across positions and scales without choosing specific observables. We introduce a higher-dimensional information lattice, an inclusion-exclusion construction that decomposes the von Neumann information of reduced density matrices into contributions labeled by subsystem position and scale. Above one dimension, overlapping subsystems can form loops, allowing information associated with one subsystem to be inferred nonlocally from the density matrices of others. The sign of the inclusion-exclusion information resolves such overlap redundancies in position and scale: positive values mark positions and scales with net information gain, while negative values occur where redundant contributions dominate new ones. We implement the construction in two dimensions and apply it to many-body ground states with distinct entanglement structures. For localized states, such as disordered-localized states or gapped ground states of local Hamiltonians, the local information decays exponentially along different directions, defining direction-dependent information length scales. In critical states with a Fermi surface, decay becomes algebraic, with dominant directions tied to the velocity of gapless modes. In the toric code, topological order manifests as a negative information value at the largest lattice scale, interpreted as global information redundancy between the smallest loop-enclosing overlapping subsystems. We also isolate the universal information carried by edge states and locate the signatures of non-Abelian fusion channels in position and scale. Our results establish the higher-dimensional information lattice as a framework for resolving universal correlation structures through the spatial and scale distribution of quantum information.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2604.11314v3
- Title: Risk-aware neural pulse compilation of continuously parameterized single-qubit gates with optimal-control benchmarking and NMR hardware validation
- Authors: Arash Fath Lipaei, Ebrahim Khaleghian, Gani Göral, Zidong Lin, Selin Aslan, Özgür E. Müstecaplıoğlu
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11314v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.11314v3.pdf

Abstract:
Pulse-level compilation can reduce the depth and calibration overhead associated with decomposing continuously parameterized quantum operations into fixed gate libraries, but conventional optimal control requires a new optimization for each target and can be sensitive to hardware uncertainty. We introduce a physics-informed neural compilation framework that learns a reusable map from continuously parameterized single-qubit gate targets directly to experimentally admissible control waveforms. The compiler is trained end-to-end through differentiable quantum dynamics without precomputed optimal-pulse labels, and its robustness is improved by first diagnosing physically relevant failure modes and then applying tail-risk-aware fine-tuning. On a three-qubit liquid-state nuclear magnetic resonance (NMR) model, the learned compiler generalizes across Haar-distributed single-qubit targets with mean nominal fidelity above 0.99 and generates new pulses in submillisecond inference time. Matched benchmarks reveal complementary advantages: robust gradient ascent pulse engineering (GRAPE) achieves the highest per-target fidelity, whereas the trained neural compiler amortizes the optimization cost across the target family, enabling substantially faster online pulse generation. Risk-aware training substantially broadens tolerance to radio-frequency (RF)-amplitude errors, including perturbations beyond the training range. Experiments on a three-spin NMR processor reproduce the predicted broadening of the RF-tolerance response, although the absolute experimental correlations are lower than the simulated values. These results establish a general workflow combining amortized machine learning, physical sensitivity diagnosis, risk-aware control, optimal-control benchmarking, and hardware testing, providing a route toward fast and robust pulse-level compilation for repeatedly varying quantum operations.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2605.00807v2
- Title: Probability distribution analysis of the cascaded variational quantum eigensolver
- Authors: Yi-Hua Lai, John P. T. Stenger, Gloria Bazargan, Igor V. Schweigert, Daniel Gunlycke
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00807v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.00807v2.pdf

Abstract:
The cascaded variational quantum eigensolver (CVQE) circumvents the need for iterative communication between the quantum and classical processing units that is necessary in the conventional VQE algorithm. While CVQE offers complete freedom to choose the guiding state as input, not all guiding states provide both accurate solutions and efficient resource usage. Our work presents a process based on trapezoidal-state preparation for selecting guiding states that yield accurate many-electron ground-state solutions with minimal resource consumption. By analyzing state probability distributions at different stages of CVQE calculations for the collision complex mediating the $\mathrm{H}_2 + \mathrm{H}_2^+ \rightarrow \mathrm{H}_3^+ + \mathrm{H}$ bimolecular reaction, we identify stage-specific errors, trace their impact on final classical optimization, and determine the optimal guiding-state parameters for given resource constraints. Our findings uncover a critical trade-off on noisy intermediate-scale quantum (NISQ) devices, demonstrating that shallower diabatic circuits provide the best compromise among optimization efficiency, state quality, sampling cost, and signal-to-noise ratio in our system.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2605.02097v3
- Title: Separability from Multipartite Measures
- Authors: Chen-Te Ma, Ma-Ke Yuan
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.stat-mech; cs.IT; hep-th; math-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.02097v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.02097v3.pdf

Abstract:
We show that multi-invariants provide necessary and sufficient criteria for full separability in finite-dimensional multipartite systems. For tripartite three-qubit states, the third-order negativity gives an exact criterion for full separability for both pure and mixed states. We then extend the framework to four-qubit pure states, whose full characterization requires eighteen independent real parameters. We identify eighteen independent entanglement measures, consisting of six bipartite, eight tripartite, and four quadripartite measures, with the third-order negativity serving as a key separability criterion. Finally, we generalize the construction to multipartite qudit systems and discuss an application to conformal field theory.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2605.03794v2
- Title: Caustics and catastrophes in strong-field physics $-$ Picard-Lefschetz theory as a universal approach to saddle-point methods in attosecond science
- Authors: Anne Weber
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03794v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.03794v2.pdf

Abstract:
Ultrashort laser pulses on the attosecond timescale are typically achieved via high-order harmonic generation (HHG), a nonlinear process in which atoms interact with intense light fields to emit a broad spectrum of harmonics. HHG is commonly described in terms of a `quantum orbits' model based on several interfering electron trajectories, thereby incorporating both quantum-mechanical effects and an intuitive picture of classical dynamics. By tuning the parameters of the driving laser field, the interplay between these trajectories can be controlled, shaping the emitted light. Mathematically, this model expresses the harmonic response as a highly oscillatory integral. Applying saddle-point methods to this integral allows it to be decomposed into contributions from distinct saddle points of the semi-classical action, thereby linking quantum dynamics to classical trajectories. However, a general framework for applying these methods across arbitrary parameters and laser configurations has been missing.   In this thesis, we introduce Picard-Lefschetz theory and develop practical numerical methods for its application. These enable the evaluation of oscillatory integrals and identification of contributions from individual critical points.   We apply these techniques to strong-field ionisation and HHG, focusing on caustics $-$ enhancement features where trajectories coalesce and standard approximations fail. Our methods remain valid in these regions, allowing systematic analysis of parameter regimes and revealing previously inaccessible features. This work improves the understanding and control of ultrafast light-matter interactions.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2605.08251v2
- Title: A finite-shot mean-squared-error boundary for Richardson zero-noise extrapolation
- Authors: Vicenzo Scavino Alfaro
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08251v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.08251v2.pdf

Abstract:
Zero-noise extrapolation (ZNE) reduces the bias of noisy quantum-circuit measurements, but Richardson coefficients and shot splitting increase their statistical error. This paper determines the noise strength at which Richardson ZNE starts to lower the mean-squared error relative to the unmitigated estimator at a fixed shot budget $B$. The boundary is set by the balance between the leading squared-bias improvement and the leading excess-variance penalty: depending on the variance exponent of the measurement, it shrinks with $B$, becomes a budget threshold, or disappears. For fixed Richardson extrapolation with linear leading bias, the Richardson order and the shot allocation change only constants; the boundary exponent is set by the measured observable and protocol. Deterministic stabilizer measurements and nonzero-variance Hamiltonian or variational-energy measurements have boundaries that shrink as $B^{-1}$ and $B^{-1/2}$, and for finite-outcome shot measurements these two classes are the only ones. Qiskit Aer simulations of Greenberger-Horne-Zeilinger (GHZ) and quantum approximate optimization algorithm (QAOA) circuits reproduce this separation, and on IBM Quantum hardware ZNE lowers the mean-squared error at equal budget in 75 of 101 instances.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2606.03380v3
- Title: Spectral theory of energy-selective quantum search with Ising Hamiltonian phase oracles
- Authors: A. S. Plyashechnik, A. A. Zhukov, A. V. Lebedev, W. V. Pogosov
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.dis-nn
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03380v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.03380v3.pdf

Abstract:
We develop an exact spectral-response theory for the Grover-type iterate \(W_T=D_ξ\exp(-\mathrm{i} T H)\), in which the evolution generated by a diagonal Ising Hamiltonian is used directly as a continuous phase oracle. An energy-grouped recurrence and its generating-function solution show how the empirical characteristic function determines the position, width, height, and saturation time of an energy-selective resonance. For an annealed Gaussian density of states, a high-density-tail resonance containing \(M\) configurations is reached after \(Θ(\sqrt{2^n/M})\) oracle calls with success probability \(Θ(1)\), giving a quadratic query improvement over independent uniform sampling with classical energy evaluation. For correlated random Ising spectra, overlap-dependent covariances lead to a realization-dependent resonance shift with root-mean-square scale \(O(n^{-3})\), parametrically larger than the resonance width, and can also reduce the peak height. The shift is both an algorithmic detuning and a coherent probe of sample-specific spectral fluctuations whose ensemble statistics reflect the Ising overlap structure. Spectral symmetrization and iterative calibration can remove or compensate the resonance-center displacement for prescribed-energy targeting. We also clarify the relation to designed spectral filters and the precision and coherence requirements of this asymptotic primitive.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2606.21962v2
- Title: Dominant Eigenstate Isolation through Symmetry-Orbit Decoupling
- Authors: Canyu He, Chunhua Zeng, Ruyu Yang, Yongdan Yang
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21962v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.21962v2.pdf

Abstract:
We address the problem of extracting a target eigenstate from an approximate pure state. Our approach, symmetry-orbit decoupling, exploits symmetries that preserve the target state while acting differently on the residual. Averaging over the resulting orbit suppresses the target--residual coherence without reducing the target population, thereby exposing a spectral structure that is hidden in the original pure state. When the target is sufficiently separated from the residual modes, it becomes the principal mode of the orbit-averaged state and can be identified through the orbit Gram matrix. Numerical VQE simulations performed with QuESTlink for six- and eight-qubit Heisenberg and Fermi--Hubbard chains show substantial improvements in ground-state energy estimation. The remaining accuracy is limited by residual components that are indistinguishable from the target under the chosen symmetry.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2609.07645v2
- Title: Geometric inflation of deviations challenges neural quantum states in dynamics of quantum Ising models
- Authors: Wladislaw Krinitsin, Jonas B. Rigo, Mohammad Abedi, Markus Schmitt
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; physics.comp-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07645v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.07645v2.pdf

Abstract:
Neural quantum states (NQS) have emerged as a powerful framework for simulating non-equilibrium dynamics in strongly correlated quantum systems, offering scalable variational representations of highly entangled states. Yet, accurate NQS simulations have been found to be surprisingly challenging in some physical regimes of limited complexity. Here, we address paradigmatic quench dynamics of a one-dimensional quantum Ising model as a controlled benchmark. Through supervised state reconstruction we establish substantially tighter empirical upper bounds on the required parameter count than previous estimates, ruling out representational limitations as the key obstruction. Instead, we uncover a geometric inflation of small deviations as a hitherto overlooked challenge for accurate solutions of the infinitesimal time-dependent variational principle (TDVP): the dynamical rotation of the kernel of the quantum geometric tensor (QGT) can suddenly lend physical significance to previously irrelevant parameter deviations. The stability of matrix product state solutions of the same TDVP suggests that the non-linearity of the neural network ansatz is the origin of the sensitivity. These results identify QGT-null-space rotation as a geometric diagnostic of sensitive NQS dynamics and as a concrete target for improving TDVP algorithms.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2609.12204v2
- Title: Path-Degenerate Quantum Interferometry for Loss Resilience in Gravitational-Wave Detectors
- Authors: Jonas Rittmeyer, Niels Boettner, Farid Khalili, Mikhail Korobko, Roman Schnabel
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; astro-ph.IM; physics.optics
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12204v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.12204v2.pdf

Abstract:
Optical decoherence degrades quantum correlations in squeezed states of light, severely limiting the quantum-nondemolition (QND) sensitivity of gravitational-wave detectors. Here, we propose the path-degenerate quantum interferometry scheme that obviates the need for entire optical subsystems---including additional filter cavities, auxiliary parametric amplifiers, and Faraday isolators---thereby drastically reducing spatial mode mismatches while inherently integrating variational output, a long-standing theoretical proposal to further deepen the QND regime. We experimentally demonstrate a core aspect of this scheme, achieving a shot-noise-preserving signal enhancement that directly counteracts the detrimental effects of readout loss. By delivering an improved signal-to-quantum-noise ratio solely through the consolidation and reduction of currently considered optical subsystems, our approach offers a highly optimized route toward enhanced quantum-noise reduction in upcoming LIGO upgrades and next-generation gravitational-wave observatories.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2609.13923v2
- Title: Partial Stabilizer Learning under Fixed Commuting Constraints and the Absence of a Copy-Rate Discount
- Authors: Masahito Hayashi, Yimin Lu
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13923v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.13923v2.pdf

Abstract:
We consider an unknown pure stabilizer state of \(n\) qudits with a fixed prime local dimension \(p\), together with prescribed commuting Pauli observables. Rather than learning the whole state, the learner must recover only complementary stabilizers that commute with the prescribed observables, together with their eigenvalues. Once the prescribed measurement is performed, this information and the observed outcome determine the corresponding conditional stabilizer state. We compare the number \(k\) of copies available to the learner with the number \(n\) of qudits and refer to their ratio \(k/n\) as the copy rate.   Suppose that the dimension \(m\) of the requested complementary stabilizer subspace satisfies \(m=βn+o(n)\) for a fixed \(0<β\leq1\). If the copy rate converges to a value below one, the optimal exact recovery probabilities and verification scores converge to zero, both in the worst case and under the uniform prior. Conversely, if the number \(k\) of copies minus the number \(n\) of qudits tends to positive infinity, the entire stabilizer state can be identified with probability tending to one, and the requested complementary information can then be extracted.   Thus, restricting the learning target to the stabilizer information needed after the prescribed measurement yields no copy-rate discount.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.15774v2
- Title: Rate and depth of two-axis twisting by pair exchange under the self-Kerr constraint
- Authors: Qing Yu, Ying Li, Cuilu Zhai, Shiqing Tang, Wangjun Lu
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15774v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.15774v2.pdf

Abstract:
In this paper, we study two bosonic modes that exchange pairs of quanta, which realize two-axis twisting exactly: within a sector of fixed particle number the exchange operator is proportional to the two-axis twisting generator, with a fixed factor of two. Because any real nonlinear medium supplies a local Kerr nonlinearity alongside this operator, the two merge into one twisting Hamiltonian governed by a single dimensionless ratio $r=χ_s/χ_e$ of the Kerr strength to the exchange strength. Two conditions then decide how much of the ideal behavior survives this constraint, and they must be met separately. The first concerns speed: the exponential squeezing rate follows in closed form, vanishes exactly at $r=\pm1$, and recovers as the square root of the distance from that point. The second concerns depth: the smallest attainable squeezing parameter depends only on the anisotropy of the mean-field saddle, attaining the full two-axis-twisting value at $r=0$ and $r=\pm3$ regardless of the rate, so that settings sharing the same anisotropy but differing twofold in rate reach the same depth. The two conditions address different targets, and a system may squeeze quickly yet terminate at a shallow optimum; only $r=0$ is optimal in both respects. The closed-form Gaussian solution reveals a further systematic bias: because the early-time decay rate of the variance exceeds twice the exponential rate, a fit to short-time data overestimates the rate by more than a factor of two. Since an isotropic cubic nonlinearity imposes $r=1$, the value at which the rate vanishes, the two strengths must be mismatched, and a moderate mismatch already restores most of the optimal depth. Finally, the constraint is invisible at low excitation: at two quanta the Kerr term shifts both coupled states equally, so the two Hamiltonians are indistinguishable until four quanta.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.17017v2
- Title: Photon-stimulated decay of $χ$-matched ancillas causes unheralded bit flips in bosonic codes
- Authors: Aleksandr Gulkanov
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.supr-con
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17017v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17017v2.pdf

Abstract:
Bosonic codes often read out their syndrome with a transmon ancilla whose computational levels $g$ and $f$ have equal parity and matched dispersive shifts: a decay $f\to e$ is then heralded and leaves the code intact, while the direct $f\to g$ is forbidden by parity. We show that the photons of the code open this decay at first order in the ancilla's decay rate. Dressing by the code mode lets the charge noise that limits the ancilla's $T_1$ drive a photon-stimulated Raman decay $|f,n\rangle\to|g,n+1\rangle$, at $(n+1)(χ_{ge}/2g_{01})^2$ times the $f\to e$ rate for a smooth noise spectrum. When the matching is set by the detuning, the decay costs $\approxΓ_{fe}/α$ per gate and photon, whatever the coupling strength; a deliberate mismatch or matching by a drive lowers this cost, and a qubit ancilla has no such channel. The opened decay is unheralded and flips a cat with probability $\approx 1/2$; being first order, it overtakes the second-order double decay $f\to e\to g$ as the ancilla improves. In a microscopic model of the Ocelot repetition cat code, it makes the bit-flip floor of the gates grow linearly with the cat size, by a factor 1.2-1.7 from four to ten photons, which would limit the noise bias to $\sim 10^3$. The published gate data end at four photons and do not yet test this growth.

- Title: Non-invasive 2D spatial measurements of electric fields within a plasma sheath
- Authors: Mykhailo Vorobiov, Rob Behary, Luke Payne, Nicolas DeStefano, William Torg, Saskia Mordijck, Charles T. Fancher, Neel Malvania, et al.
- Categories: physics.plasm-ph (primary); physics.plasm-ph; physics.app-ph; physics.atom-ph; physics.optics; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.12322v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.12322v2.pdf

Abstract:
We introduce an all-optical quantum-enhanced diagnostic for electric fields in low-temperature plasmas. Trace amounts of rubidium vapor, added to an argon plasma, allow us to employ spectrally narrow electric field-sensitive optical resonances via the quantum optical effect of Rydberg electromagnetically induced transparency. This non-invasive technique enables measurements of electric fields in plasma with a 1~V/cm precision and a 1.5~V/cm lower detection limit. By collecting the fluorescence from the illuminated region of interest, we reconstruct a 2D spatial profile of the electric field magnitude with $56~μ$m resolution. As a proof-of-principle demonstration, we measured the changes in the electric field within the plasma sheath surrounding a biased Langmuir probe tip. This method holds significant potential for studying sheath structures in low-temperature plasmas.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2608.14816v2
- Title: Universal Emergence of Bosonic and Fermionic Algebras in a Deterministic Proper-Time Framework
- Authors: Alessio Maiezza
- Categories: hep-th (primary); hep-th; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14816v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.14816v2.pdf

Abstract:
This work develops the deterministic, discrete proper-time framework introduced in our previous work, Eur.\ Phys.\ J.\ C \textbf{86} (2026) 829, in which quantum field theory emerges as an effective infrared description characterized by a running Planck constant. There, the effective quantization scale was inferred from the microscopic multiplicity unresolved by coarse-graining. Here, we provide its dynamical and operatorial realization and extend the construction to fermionic degrees of freedom. First, consistency under changes of macroscopic resolution leads to the structure of the Renormalization Group Equation, while the running Planck constant governs the crossover toward the deterministic regime. Second, we represent the reversible microscopic dynamics through finite-difference translations in field-configuration space. After coarse-graining, the resulting operator-valued canonical commutation relations reproduce the same quantization scale previously obtained from statistical microstate counting, thereby linking microscopic evolution to the emergent canonical algebra. Third, representing the same update within a Grassmann algebra yields the corresponding canonical anticommutation relations. The bosonic and fermionic sectors thus inherit a common effective Planck constant without introducing an independent fermionic update or quantization scale. Finally, we discuss possible implications for high-energy loop amplitudes and effective Hawking radiation.

- arXiv: 2610.03870v2
- Title: Time-dependent integrable quantum field theories with dynamical boundaries
- Authors: Pradip Kattel
- Categories: math-ph (primary); math-ph; cond-mat.str-el; hep-th; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03870v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.03870v2.pdf

Abstract:
We present a general method to construct and solve time-dependent integrable chiral quantum field theories on finite intervals from autonomous scattering and reflection data. Spatial homogeneity forces the spectral labels of right- and left-moving particles to be affine with a common slope, so bulk and boundary couplings sample one spectral trajectory. A complete round trip shifts the spectral labels and gives boundary quantum Knizhnik--Zamolodchikov equations. The Yang--Baxter and reflection equations provide local consistency, while boundary-qKZ flatness provides global consistency. We reconstruct the local Hamiltonian by Cayley inversion of the bulk and boundary matching matrices. We apply the construction to rational $SU(N)$ scattering with fundamental boundary impurities and to an $O(3)$ model with a spin-$\tfrac12$ impurity. For the $SU(2)$ impurity, we construct explicit boundary-qKZ solutions as lattice sums of off-shell boundary Bethe vectors and recover the static open-chain Bethe equations when the drive rate vanishes

- Title: Non-Monotonic Exciton Transport Efficiency in Multi-layer Ring-shaped Quantum Networks inspired by Photosystem I
- Authors: Yi Li, Rui Li, Kai-Ya Zhang, Ming-Zi Jin, Tao Liu, Qing Ai
- Categories: cond-mat.soft (primary); cond-mat.soft; physics.bio-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13949v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.13949v2.pdf

Abstract:
Photosystem I (PSI) is one of the most efficient natural photosynthetic complexes and serves as a structural prototype for understanding excitation energy transfer (EET) and designing artificial light-harvesting systems. Here, we construct a multilayer elliptical-conical quantum network inspired by PSI to investigate how antenna architecture affects EET. Excitation dynamics are simulated using the quantum jump approach combined with coherent modified Redfield theory (QJA-CMRT). The transfer efficiency exhibits a pronounced nonmonotonic dependence on pigment density, reaching a maximum of 0.86 at x=6 in the five-layer network. The optimal configuration contains 91 pigments, close to the number of core antenna pigments in natural PSI. Our combined density-of-states and kinetic analyses reveal that this nonmonotonic dependence originates from a trade-off: at low pigment densities, enhanced band overlap promotes energy transfer, whereas at high densities, excessive exciton delocalization compromises funnel directionality and reduces efficiency; the optimal configuration is consequently achieved at x=6. These findings provide insights into PSI antenna organization and offer guidelines for designing efficient artificial light-harvesting systems.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-19 14:00
- arXiv: 2610.16864v2
- Title: Exact Gaussian--Hermite closure of the mapping quantum--classical Liouville equation
- Authors: Sahand Nikzat Ali Nassimi, Rony Modak Parai
- Categories: physics.chem-ph (primary); physics.chem-ph; physics.comp-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16864v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.16864v2.pdf

Abstract:
In the Meyer--Miller--Stock--Thoss mapping representation, the Liouville operator of the quantum--classical Liouville equation contains an excess coupling term in addition to the Poisson bracket. Neglecting this term yields the Poisson bracket mapping equation (PBME). We expand the mapping dependence of the density in Hermite functions about the Gaussian factor common to all physical mapping densities. In this basis, the excess coupling term acts purely as a raising operator and exactly cancels the raising contribution generated by the back--reaction force. Consequently, the exact dynamics does not generate higher Hermite orders and closes on a finite, sparse system of equations for the coefficient fields. Within the physical sector, these equations reduce to the subsystem quantum--classical Liouville equation. In contrast, the PBME retains the raising contribution of the force, modifying the back reaction within the physical sector while continuously generating higher Hermite orders. For Tully's dual avoided crossing model, direct propagation of the coefficient equations confirms this finite closure and agrees with independent solutions of the subsystem equation. Comparison with converged full quantum dynamics further shows that, across all regimes examined, the PBME is less accurate than the quantum--classical description for populations, coherences, and momentum distributions.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2610.26900v1
- Title: Concurrent Entanglement Purification Scheduling in Dynamic Quantum Networks
- Authors: Gongyu Ni, Lester Ho
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26900v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26900v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum networks must deliver end-to-end entanglement whose fidelity meets application requirements, which typically requires entanglement purification along multi-hop paths. Existing scheduling approaches either purify at the link level or optimize end-to-end fidelity for a static set of requests, and do not capture concurrent requests competing for network resources over time. This paper studies concurrent entanglement purification scheduling in a time-slotted quantum network with heterogeneous links, in which requests compete for link capacity and quantum memories, and each request may be served by multiple parallel paths for increased reliability. We formulate a mixed-integer linear programming (MILP) scheduler that maximizes the number of scheduled requests, with max-min path allocation as a fairness criterion, and develop reinforcement learning (RL) schedulers based on DQN, PPO, and SAC, whose rewards balance request success against Bell-pair consumption. Simulations under varying network load, topologies and target fidelity thresholds show that the RL schedulers achieve the highest success rates, improving on MILP by up to 6% and on Progressive Filling and Greedy heuristics by 16.5% and 19.5% respectively, at the cost of higher Bell pair consumption and the deferral of a small fraction of requests. MILP, in contrast, achieves the highest fairness and the lowest Bell pair consumption in most scenarios.
