- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2511.03846v3
- Title: Temporal entanglement transition in chaotic quantum many-body dynamics
- Authors: Ilya Vilkoviskiy, Michael Sonner, Qi Camm Huang, Wen Wei Ho, Alessio Lerose, Dmitry A. Abanin
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.stat-mech; cond-mat.str-el
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.03846v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.03846v3.pdf

Abstract:
Temporal entanglement (TE) of an influence matrix (IM) has been proposed as a measure of complexity of simulating dynamics of local observables in a many-body system. Foligno et al. [Phys. Rev. X 13, 041008 (2023)] recently argued that the TE in chaotic 1d quantum circuits obeys linear (volume-law) scaling with evolution time. To reconcile this apparent high complexity of IM with the rapid thermalization of local observables, here we study the relation between TE, non-Markovianity, and local temporal correlations for chaotic quantum baths. By exactly solving a random-unitary bath model, and bounding distillable entanglement between future and past degrees of freedom, we identify a regime where TE is extensive and reflects genuine non-Markovianity. This memory, however, is entirely contained in highly complex temporal correlations, and its effect on few-point temporal correlators is negligible. An IM coarse-graining procedure, reducing the allowed frequency of measurements of the probe system, results in a transition from volume- to {subvolume- (area or logarithmic)} law TE scaling. We demonstrate the generality of this TE transition in 1d circuits by analyzing the kicked Ising model analytically at dual-unitary points, as well as numerically away from them. This finding indicates that dynamics of local observables are fully captured by a subvolume-law IM. We provide evidence that the compact IM MPS obtained via standard compression algorithms accurately describes local~evolution.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2512.02239v2
- Title: Entanglement spectrum and Schmidt mode evolution during two-particle scattering
- Authors: Arsam Najafian, Mark Van Raamsdonk
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; hep-th
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.02239v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.02239v2.pdf

Abstract:
A particle initially in a pure state but interacting with some environment evolves into a discrete ensemble of pure states, the eigenstates of its reduced density operator, with ensemble probabilities given by the corresponding eigenvalues. In this work, we use numerics to present explicit results for the time-dependence of these eigenvalues and eigenstates for simple scattering experiments in one and two dimensions. This provides a time-resolved picture of the scattering process, showing in detail how an initial state described entirely in terms of continuous parameters evolves into a discrete set of possible outcomes, each with an associated probability and time-evolving wavefunction. We find that for scattering of Gaussian wavepackets in one dimension, the late time spectrum is dominated by two large eigenvalues nearly equal to the transmission and reflection probabilities associated with the central value of momentum. The corresponding eigenstates appear as single-peaked reflected or transmitted wavepackets. The remaining smaller eigenvalues, which increase to a maximum during scattering and then decrease to small values, correspond to reflected or transmitted wavepackets with multiple spatially separated parts. In this case and also for two-dimensional scattering, we find that successively smaller eigenvalues correspond to probability distributions with successively more peaks. These multi-peaked states correspond to outcomes of the scattering experiment where a particle initially in a single wavepacket ends up in a superposition of separated wavepackets after scattering.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2512.21848v2
- Title: A General Framework for Constructing Local Hidden-state Models to Determine the Steerability
- Authors: Yanning Jia, Fenzhuo Guo, Mengyan Li, Haifeng Dong, Fei Gao
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.21848v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.21848v2.pdf

Abstract:
Not all entangled states can exhibit quantum steering, and determining whether a given entangled state is steerable is a crucial problem in quantum information theory. The main challenge lies in verifying the existence of a local hidden-state (LHS) model capable of reproducing all post-measurement assemblages generated by arbitrary measurements. To address this, we propose a machine learning-based framework that employs batch sampling of measurements and gradient-based optimization to construct an optimal LHS model. We validate our method by analyzing the steerability of two-qubit Werner and two-qutrit isotropic states. For Werner states, our approach saturates the analytical visibility bounds under three Pauli measurements, arbitrary projective measurements (PVMs), and arbitrary positive operator-valued measurements (POVMs). For isotropic states, we achieve the known analytical bounds under arbitrary PVMs. We further investigate the steerability of this class of states under arbitrary POVMs, and our results suggest that POVMs can offer an advantage over PVMs in revealing the steerability of such states.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2512.23917v2
- Title: Tensor computing application programming interface: An application-oriented, lightweight API for portable high-performance tensor network computations
- Authors: Rong-Yang Sun, Tomonori Shirakawa, Hidehiko Kohshiro, D. N. Sheng, Seiji Yunoki
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.str-el; cs.LG
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.23917v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.23917v2.pdf

Abstract:
Tensor networks (TNs) are a central computational tool in quantum science and artificial intelligence. However, the lack of a unified software interface across tensor-computing frameworks severely limits the portability of TN applications, coupling algorithmic development to specific hardware and software back ends. To address this challenge, we introduce the Tensor Computing Application Programming Interface (TCAPI)---an application-oriented, lightweight API designed to enable framework-independent, high-performance TN applications, currently specified for \texttt{C++} and \texttt{Python}. TCAPI provides well-defined type-system conventions that abstract tensor objects, together with a minimal yet expressive set of core functions for essential tensor manipulations and tensor linear-algebra operations, with operation semantics shared across language specifications. Through numerical demonstrations of representative tensor-network applications implemented in \texttt{C++} using TCAPI, we show that code written against TCAPI can be migrated seamlessly across heterogeneous hardware and software platforms while achieving performance comparable to native framework implementations. We further provide open-source implementations of TCAPI based on \textit{Cytnx} and \textit{ITensor} in \texttt{C++}, and on \textit{NumPy} in \texttt{Python}, demonstrating its practicality and ease of integration with existing tensor-computing frameworks.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2604.00656v2
- Title: Quantum Algorithms for Gibbs Expectation of Non-log-concave and Heavy-tailed Distributions
- Authors: Xinmiao Li, Jin-Peng Liu
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00656v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.00656v2.pdf

Abstract:
We establish a systematic framework of unbiased quantum sampling and estimation protocols for the classical Gibbs expectation. This framework generalizes existing approaches to the partition function estimation and has broader applications in various fields. We consider sampling and estimation for a wide class of non-log-concave distributions, particularly heavy-tailed ones, under relaxed assumptions beyond strong convexity, such as dissipativity.   We develop an unbiased extension of quantum-accelerated multilevel Monte Carlo (QA-MLMC) to eliminate all biases from discretization and time truncation, together with introducing a change-of-measure approach and the Girsanov theorem via Radon-Nikodym derivatives. As a result, our approach achieves quantum complexity $\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(ε^{-1})$ within error $ε$, whereas the classical MLMC requires $\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(ε^{-2})$ and existing quantum algorithms yield biased estimators under stronger assumptions. Furthermore, our unified framework enables unbiased quantum sampling and estimation for certain heavy-tailed distributions after transformation. We provide several concrete applications of our approach in statistics, machine learning, and finance, towards more practical scenarios of the quantum acceleration of stochastic processes.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2604.02416v2
- Title: Scalable Determination of Penalization Weights for Constrained Optimizations on Approximate Solvers
- Authors: Edoardo Alessandroni, Sergi Ramos-Calderer, Michel Krispin, Markus Kirsch, Christian Münch, Fritz Schinkel, Stefan Walter, Martin Kliesch, et al.
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02416v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.02416v2.pdf

Abstract:
Quadratic unconstrained binary optimization (QUBO) provides problem formulations for various computational problems that can be solved with dedicated QUBO solvers, based on classical or quantum computation. A common approach to constrained combinatorial optimization problems is to enforce the constraints in the QUBO formulation by adding penalization terms. Penalization introduces an additional hyperparameter that significantly affects the solver's efficacy: the relative weight between the objective terms and the penalization terms. Here we study the problem of a priori determining suitable penalization weights from a foundational and practical perspective. We formally establish that finding its optimal value for Gibbs solvers is $\mathsf{NP}$-hard even approximately and $\#\mathsf{P}$-hard exactly. This serves us as a motivation to develop a widely applicable heuristic strategy for determining suitable penalization weights, with provable guarantees for Gibbs solvers, and polynomial complexity for broad problem classes. Experiments across diverse problems and solver architectures, including large-scale instances on Fujitsu's Digital Annealer, show robust performance and order-of-magnitude speedups over existing heuristics. We further extend the strategy to quantum approximate optimization and demonstrate its performance on Quantinuum's H2-2 trapped-ion processor.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2605.18243v3
- Title: One pure steered state implies Einstein-Podolsky-Rosen steering for two-qubit entangled states
- Authors: Qiu-Cheng Song, Joonwoo Bae
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18243v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.18243v3.pdf

Abstract:
In this work, we show that a two-qubit entangled state admitting at least one pure steered state is Einstein-Podolsky-Rosen (EPR) steerable from Alice to Bob. Pure steered states signifies that the quantum steering ellipsoid of Bob is tangent to his Bloch sphere at least at a single point. Furthermore, we prove that for a two-qubit entangled state, Bob's quantum steering ellipsoid is tangent to his Bloch sphere at exactly $N$ points, for $N\in \{ 0,1,2,\infty\}$, if and only if Alice's quantum steering ellipsoid is tangent to her Bloch sphere at exactly $N$ points. For any two-qubit entangled state, therefore, if one party can steer the other to at least one pure state, the state is two-way EPR steerable. We also present several illuminating examples of two-qubit entangled states such that the EPR steering can be verified in terms of pure steered states. Our result addresses the Gisin theorem in a EPR steering scenario: at least a single pure steered state implies two-way steering.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2605.20078v2
- Title: On Performance and Limitations of Quantum Wave Packet Dynamics Simulated using NISQ Hardware
- Authors: Tamila Kuanysheva, Jonathan Andrade-Plascencia, Jayakrushna Sahoo, Brian Kendrick, Dmitri Babikov
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20078v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.20078v2.pdf

Abstract:
We investigate one-dimensional quantum wave-packet dynamics using a grid-based encoding of the wave function onto qubit registers. Time evolution is implemented using a split operator approach, with the kinetic energy operator realized via the Quantum Fourier Transform (QFT) and arbitrary discretized potentials represented by commuting Pauli-Z gates. The algorithm is benchmarked against classical grid-based simulations and validated using a noiseless Qiskit Statevector simulator before being implemented on current quantum hardware (IBM Quantum and IonQ) for qubit counts ranging from two to five. Good agreement with benchmark calculations is obtained for two- and three-qubit implementations, while deviations increase at larger qubit counts due to hardware noise and increased circuit depth. Among the devices examined, the algorithm maintains closer agreement with the benchmark on the trapped-ion IonQ Forte processor as the number of qubits increases.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2605.22226v2
- Title: Geometric Construction of Optimal Teleportation Witnesses
- Authors: Yanning Jia, Fenzhuo Guo, Mengxuan Bai, Mengyan Li, Haifeng Dong, Fei Gao
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22226v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.22226v2.pdf

Abstract:
Not all entangled states are useful for quantum teleportation. We present a geometric method to construct optimal teleportation witnesses, which provide operational necessary and sufficient criteria for identifying the teleportation usefulness of arbitrary two-qudit entangled states. Specifically, by developing a two-layer iterative cutting-plane algorithm to solve the shortest distance problem from the target state $ρ$ to the convex set $S$ of useless states, we obtain the projection point $σ^* \in S$ and then construct the optimal teleportation witness from the projection geometry. Moreover, the shortest distance $D(ρ)$ obtained during this construction also serves as a necessary and sufficient criterion for usefulness. We apply our method to identify the teleportation usefulness of three classes of entangled states.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2605.25066v2
- Title: QML-PipeGuard: Detecting Channel-Substitution Attacks on Quantum Machine Learning Pipelines in Untrusted Clouds
- Authors: Esra Yeniaras
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.CR; cs.LG
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25066v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.25066v2.pdf

Abstract:
As machine-learning workloads move onto quantum hardware they inherit a trust problem classical AI deployments have largely solved and quantum ones have not: quantum machine learning (QML) is already sold as a cloud service, and a customer cannot check that the circuit they paid for is actually the circuit that ran. We show the gap is exploitable. A malicious or cost-cutting provider can substitute the declared quantum channel with one that reproduces every accuracy test the customer performs, passes the single-observable checks used in practice, and still differs from the declared computation at the channel level. We call this a channel-substitution attack and instantiate it in three deployments: audit evasion, service downgrade, and result forgery in reproducibility review. QML-PipeGuard detects the attack at runtime from measurement statistics the customer already collects. Its guarantee is two-sided, stated in the distance a local verifier measures: any substitution shifting a single-qubit output marginal by $δ$ in trace norm forces a contract deviation of at least $δ/\sqrt{3}$, the constant tight and independent of qubit count; the guarantee is relative to the chosen observable family and reference-input set. For local-unitary substitutions the adversary's blind set is characterised exactly, as a commutation condition, which gives the defender a rule for choosing challenge inputs that shrinks it. A finite-shot budget rule bounds false accepts and false alarms at once, one tolerance separates attack from the benign drift observed here, and every decision enters a hash-anchored audit trail. On an IBM Heron r2 processor a substituted quantum-kernel intrusion-detection pipeline evades the weak contract and our verifier halts it, while the honest channel stays within tolerance.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2606.29854v2
- Title: Higher-Order Magnus Corrections to Leakage Elimination Operator-Based Quantum Control Pulse Design in the Low-Frequency Regime
- Authors: Hang Yu, Kai-Yu Yuan, Feng-Hua Ren, Zhao-Ming Wang
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29854v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.29854v2.pdf

Abstract:
Effective quantum control is essential for quantum computing and information processing, yet level leakage remains a bottleneck limiting control fidelity. The leakage elimination operator (LEO) approach is highly effective at suppressing leakage from a target subspace to other subspaces. Analytical control conditions under the high-frequency driving limit have been derived via the Feshbach PQ partitioning technique. However, the driving intensity is subject to fundamental physical bounds, and low-frequency driving is experimentally more feasible. In this work, we reformulate LEO within the Magnus framework and extend it beyond the high-frequency limit. We show that the analytical control conditions obtained from the Feshbach PQ partitioning technique are equivalent to the zeroth-order Magnus expansion. We then analyze the higher-order Magnus corrections. By minimizing the leakage, we obtain a modified pulse condition suitable for the low-frequency regime. As an illustrative example, we study near-perfect state transfer through a one-dimensional spin chain under LEO control. We find that the second-order Magnus correction improves the transmission fidelity at low-frequency driving, while the improvement becomes negligible at high frequencies. Our results provide an effective route for designing control pulses in the low-frequency regime, with potential applications in superconducting qubits, trapped ions, and solid-state spin systems.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2608.13561v2
- Title: Proof-of-Principle Implementation of Quantum Key Distribution in an Indefinite Causal Order
- Authors: Yann Valibouse, Martí Cladera-Rosselló, Michael Antesberger, Hector Spencer-Wood, Kyrylo Simonov, Patrik Sund, Mathieu Bozzio, Philip Walther, et al.
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13561v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.13561v2.pdf

Abstract:
In quantum physics, the order in which different operations occur can be placed in superposition. The resulting processes have an indefinite causal order, which is of fundamental interest and can be viewed as a novel quantum resource enabling new protocols. Here, we report an experimental proof-of-principle implementation of one such protocol, where we perform BB84-like quantum cryptography by placing \AB's measurement-and-preparation operations in a photonic quantum SWITCH. By embedding \AB\ within the quantum SWITCH, the protocol achieves an average eavesdropper detection probability of $0.15 \pm 0.02$ per shared qubit, with eavesdropper detection performed through measurements of the control qubit rather than comparing the key. Unlike standard BB84 and related schemes, which detect eavesdropping by publicly revealing and discarding a fraction of the raw key, our approach requires no disclosure of key material: every retained qubit can, in principle, be tested for eavesdropping while remaining available for key generation. Although the present implementation does not yet constitute a secure quantum key distribution protocol, owing to post-selection required for measurements within the quantum SWITCH, it provides a proof of principle that indefinite causal order can be exploited to detect eavesdropping without sacrificing key bits.

- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20297v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.20297v2.pdf

Abstract:
Aaronson and Ambainis (Theory of Computing, 2014) conjectured that quantum query algorithms admit efficient almost-everywhere classical simulation: for any $T$-query quantum algorithm, its acceptance probability can be approximated on a $(1-δ)$ fraction of inputs, up to $ε$ additive error, using $\mathrm{poly}(T, 1/ε, 1/δ)$ classical queries. At a high level, the conjecture suggests that exponential quantum speedups are possible only on sufficiently structured inputs.   In this work, we make progress on this conjecture by proving it for quantum algorithms that make massively parallel quantum queries. In contrast, Yamakawa and Zhandry (Journal of the ACM, 2024) showed that quantum algorithms restricted to parallel queries can still achieve exponential speedups over classical algorithms for sampling problems. We establish our simulation theorem by proving the stronger statement that parallel-query quantum algorithms cannot distinguish the uniform distribution over oracles from oracles drawn from so-called "dense distributions". Our main technical contribution is a coupling theorem that relates the uniform distribution over oracles to oracles drawn from dense distributions.   We further extend this approach beyond the purely parallel setting. We prove simulation theorems for quantum algorithms with constant rounds of adaptivity, and for quantum algorithms with a bounded quantum-query prefix followed by a massively parallel quantum-query stage.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2608.23159v2
- Title: Taming Spacetime Overhead and Design Complexity in Distributed Fault-Tolerant Superconducting Quantum Computation
- Authors: Qinjing Yu, Ke Liu
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23159v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.23159v2.pdf

Abstract:
Scaling fault-tolerant superconducting quantum computers will likely require distributed architectures built from multiple manufacturable quantum processing units. A central question is whether noisy and slow inter-chip operations impose substantial spacetime overhead or orchestration burdens compared with monolithic architectures. To answer this question, we present a hardware-grounded architectural co-design together with a comprehensive resource-estimation protocol for surface-code-based modular processors. The design confines inter-chip latency and noise to module boundaries, preventing slow, noisy links from inducing prohibitive spacetime overhead or becoming a global orchestration bottleneck. The resource-estimation protocol integrates hardware constraints and circuit-level error-correction performance into utility-scale algorithmic cost estimates, enabling a controlled assessment of different architectures. Using RSA-2048 factorization as a demanding benchmark, we estimate resources under experimentally anchored parameters and realistic superconducting hardware constraints. Compared with a large, ideal monolithic baseline, the resulting distributed architecture requires only modest additional resource overhead in both qubit count and execution time. More importantly, the overhead is nearly scale-invariant across a broad module-capacity window, decoupling chip size from global performance. This decoupling turns module capacity from a finely tuned architectural parameter into a flexible engineering degree of freedom, allowing chip sizes to be set by manufacturability and control-packaging constraints rather than architectural fine-tuning. These results establish a viable route to distributed fault-tolerant superconducting quantum computation that scales without prohibitive resource growth or heavy orchestration burden.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2609.07730v2
- Title: Propaq: A Python package for Heisenberg Propagation
- Authors: Hrishikesh Belagali, Ryan LaRose
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07730v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.07730v2.pdf

Abstract:
We introduce Propaq, a performant and flexible Python package implementing Heisenberg propagation techniques for classically simulating quantum circuits. Propaq is compatible with major quantum software packages like Qiskit and Cirq, and matches or outperforms similar state-of-the-art packages for Heisenberg propagation. Beyond this, Propaq has multiple distinguishing features for simulation and research. These include Heisenberg propagation in any basis, hybrid Schrödinger-Heisenberg simulation, custom truncation and noisy simulation methods, and detailed logging information for analyzing simulation accuracy, runtime, and memory usage. We describe these features, how to use Propaq for research, and its backend implementation.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2609.12346v2
- Title: The Low-Individual-Degree Test Without the Diagonal-Lines Test Is Not Quantum-Sound
- Authors: Honghao Fu, Nengkun Yu, Tianrun Zhao
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.CC
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12346v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.12346v2.pdf

Abstract:
To prove the quantum soundness of the classical low-individual-degree test, the authors of \cite{JNVWY20LID} defined three subtests, namely the axis-parallel lines test, the self-consistency test, and the diagonal-lines test. An interesting question is whether the diagonal-lines test can be removed. In this paper, we show that the diagonal-lines test cannot simply be removed without another compatibility mechanism. Consequently, replacing the "conditional linear functions" by "coordinate deletion functions" in the proof of MIP*=RE, as mentioned in \cite{JNVWY20LID}, does not by itself preserve the required soundness.   The authors of \cite{JNVWY20LID} found an example that requires the diagonal-lines test when \((m, d, q) = (2, 2, 4)\); we give an example when \((m, d) = (2, 2)\) and \(q\) is any odd prime.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2610.18225v2
- Title: PPVM: A Circuit Simulation Framework for Digital Twins of Quantum Processors
- Authors: Xiu-Zhe Luo, David Plankensteiner, Kai-Hsin Wu, Rob Patterson, Alexander Schuckert, Oriol Rubies-Bigorda, Refaat Ismail, Jonhas Colina, et al.
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18225v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18225v2.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum computers are approaching the early fault-tolerant regime. Verifying the next generation of quantum hardware requires joint emulation of the quantum processor, hardware noise, and classical control and decoding logic. We introduce the Programmable Pauli Virtual Machine (PPVM), an open-source framework for digital twins of quantum processors. PPVM unifies Pauli propagation and generalized tableau simulation under a common abstraction within a single virtual machine. Its bytecode supports arbitrary classical control flow, with measurement-conditioned feed-forward available in the generalized tableau backend. For neutral-atom hardware, we derive independent and correlated atom-loss channels and integrate them into both backends. We demonstrate loss-aware magic state distillation on over a thousand physical qubits and integration with classical decoding through Bloqade QEC Studio.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2610.24342v2
- Title: Quantum-Classical Iterative Refinement for the Pressure-Poisson Equation on NISQ Hardware
- Authors: Doyeol "David" Ahn, Byeongyong Park, Rho Shin Myong, Franz Klein
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24342v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.24342v2.pdf

Abstract:
We present a hybrid quantum-classical iterative refinement for the pressure-Poisson equation of the incompressible Navier-Stokes projection method on noisy quantum hardware. Rather than solving the pressure equation once per time step, we solve it repeatedly for the normalized residual and scale each quantum direction by a least-squares fit to the physical residual. As a result, the error of the quantum solve enters as a contraction factor $\sinθ_k$ of the residual rather than as an error floor. This removes the requirement of a state infidelity below $10^{-7}$ that was estimated for single-solve schemes. The hardware noise then sets the rate of convergence but not the attainable accuracy, and it is this change of the role of the quantum solve, not an improvement of the hardware, that allows the residual to converge. With a boundary-consistent staggered (MAC) discretization, the pressure residual equals the velocity divergence left after projection up to the factor $Δt/ρ$. On the IBM processor ibm_cleveland, a four-qubit ($16\times16$) problem reached a relative residual below $10^{-7}$ within eight to ten corrections in all 20 runs of a pre-registered experiment, whereas a single solve with a comparable number of shots stopped at 0.33-0.59. In a six-qubit ($64\times64$) pilot, all three runs reached the same tolerance in 13 corrections. On the IonQ Forte processor, a manufactured-solution benchmark reached $5.6\times10^{-8}$ in nine corrections. With the variational training itself carried out on ibm_cleveland, the residual fell to $5.6\times10^{-4}$ in seven corrections, below the pre-specified tolerance of $10^{-3}$, at the rate predicted by a noise model. To the best of our knowledge, these are the first converged pressure solutions obtained on quantum hardware.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2601.00442v3
- Title: The JLMS formula in a large code with approximate error correction
- Authors: Xi Dong, Donald Marolf, Pratik Rath
- Categories: hep-th (primary); hep-th; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.00442v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.00442v3.pdf

Abstract:
Gauge/gravity duality is often described as a quantum error correcting code. However, as seen in the Jafferis-Lewkowycz-Maldacena-Suh (JLMS) formula, exact quantum error correction with complementary recovery (and thus entanglement wedge reconstruction) emerges only in the limit $G \to 0$. As a result, precise arguments controlling error terms have focused on what we call `small' codes which, as $G \to 0$, describe only perturbative excitations near a given classical solution. Such settings are quite restrictive and, in particular, they prohibit discussion of any modular flow that would change the classical background. As a result, they forbid consideration of modular flows generated by semiclassical bulk states at order-one modular parameters.   In contrast, we present a single `large' code for the bulk theory that can accommodate such flows and, in particular, in the $G \to 0$ limit includes superpositions of states associated with distinct classical backgrounds. This large code is assembled from small codes that each satisfy an approximate Faulkner-Lewkowycz-Maldacena formula. In this extended setting we clarify the meaning of the (approximate) JLMS relation between bulk and boundary modular Hamiltonians and quantify its validity in an appropriate class of states.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2602.15987v2
- Title: Gaussian continuous tensor network states: short-distance properties and imaginary-time evolution
- Authors: Marco Rigobello, Erez Zohar
- Categories: hep-th (primary); hep-th; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15987v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.15987v2.pdf

Abstract:
We study Gaussian continuous tensor network states (GCTNS) - a finitely parameterized subclass of Gaussian states admitting an interpretation as continuum limits of discrete tensor network states. We show that, at short distance, GCTNS correspond to vacua of free Lifshitz theories with even dynamical exponent, establishing a connection between certain entanglement properties of the two. Two schemes for approximating ground states of free bosonic field theories using GCTNS are presented: rational approximants to the exact dispersion relation and Trotterized imaginary-time evolution. We apply them to Klein-Gordon theory and characterize the resulting GCTNS, identifying the scale at which deviations from its conformal short-distance behavior appear. In particular, we construct an optimal rational approximation for which, at fixed accuracy, the bond dimension grows only logarithmically with the UV cutoff. These results provide a simple and analytically controlled setting to assess the strengths and limitations of GCTNS as variational ansätze for relativistic quantum fields.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2606.30222v2
- Title: Multiparameter Quantum Estimation and Degeneracy Structure in Three-Flavor Neutrino Oscillations
- Authors: Bhavna Yadav, Amir Subba, Yu Shi
- Categories: hep-ph (primary); hep-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30222v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.30222v2.pdf

Abstract:
We investigate three-flavor neutrino oscillations within the framework of quantum estimation theory using the quantum Fisher information matrix (QFIM). We analyze all six independent elements of the QFIM for the parameters $θ_{23}$, $δ_{\mathrm{CP}}$, and $Δm^2_{31}$, together with the corresponding quantum Cramér--Rao bounds and normalized parameter correlations. To connect the intrinsic quantum sensitivity of the neutrino state with experimentally accessible information, we further compare the QFI with the classical Fisher information (CFI) associated with flavor-probability measurements. We find that the difference between the quantum and classical information bounds is strongly dependent on the oscillation parameter and $L/E$, demonstrating that the efficiency with which the available quantum information is accessed depends on the measurement. We also investigate probability-degenerate solutions in the $(θ_{23},δ_{\mathrm{CP}})$ parameter space. Although different parameter sets can produce identical $ν_μ\rightarrowν_e$ appearance probabilities at specific $L/E$ values, their corresponding quantum states can remain distinct, as demonstrated by the quantum fidelity. The QFIM differences further reveal distinct local quantum-information geometries for these probability-degenerate solutions, while the corresponding CFIM differences show a parameter and $L/E$ dependent degree of distinguishability accessible through the flavor-probability measurement. Our results demonstrate that QFI and CFI provide complementary information for neutrino parameter estimation, with the QFIM serving as a measurement-independent quantum benchmark and the CFI characterizing the information accessible through a specified measurement.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2607.06050v2
- Title: Genuine Multi-Entropy in the Toric Code
- Authors: Sriram Akella, Norihiro Iizuka, Akihiro Miyata
- Categories: hep-th (primary); hep-th; cond-mat.str-el; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06050v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.06050v2.pdf

Abstract:
We study genuine multi-entropy as a diagnostic of multipartite entanglement in the toric code, which provides a controlled setting for probing multipartite structures in topologically ordered states. Our main question is whether genuine multi-entropy captures information that is not reducible to conventional lower-party entropic data, such as topological entanglement entropy. We first analyze toric-code ground states that admit a stabilizer-state description, where the relevant quantities can be evaluated exactly. We conjecture that, for stabilizer states and $\mathtt{q}\ge4$, the $\mathtt{q}$-partite genuine multi-entropy at replica index $n<\mathtt{q}$ collapses to a linear combination of multi-entropies involving at most $\mathtt{q}-2$ parties. We establish this pattern explicitly for $\mathtt{q}=4$ in the toric code stabilizer sector: for $n=2,3$, the genuine multi-entropy is proportional to the tripartite information $I_3$ and, for the Kitaev-Preskill partition, contains no independent genuine four-partite information beyond that captured by the topological entanglement entropy. At $n=4$, however, this reduction breaks down: the genuine multi-entropy is no longer proportional to $I_3$, but remains a topological invariant of the toric-code stabilizer ground states under Kitaev-Preskill-preserving deformations that do not change whether the numbers of vertex- and plaquette-type junctions coincide. For generic non-stabilizer superpositions within the ground-state manifold and for coherent superpositions of local excitations, the low-$n$ reduction also fails. These results show that genuine multi-entropy probes multipartite entanglement structure beyond the tripartite information, and hence beyond the topological entanglement entropy in the Kitaev-Preskill partition, whereas for stabilizer states at low replica index it reduces to lower-partite entropic data.

- Title: Ising-machine-like Driving Condition Restrictions in SSBM and Additional Pseudo-annealing- Driving Method for SSBM that Achieves Best Cut Value Search -
- Authors: Toshiya Sato
- Categories: physics.optics (primary); physics.optics; math-ph; nlin.AO; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12545v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.12545v3.pdf

Abstract:
In our previous work, we proposed and experimentally demonstrated a physics-based simulator for combinatorial optimization problems (SSBM), which exploits a distinctive spontaneous symmetry breaking (SSB) phenomenon identified in a dissipative system exhibiting robust causality described by an iterative equation. We further conducted numerical simulations in which initial fluctuations of distinct patterns were applied to large-scale benchmark problems, and reported that the SSBM exhibits a unique behavior absent in conventional Ising machines: under specific conditions, pseudo-spin patterns progressively converge during the solution-search process and ultimately coalesce into a single pattern, with the cut value of the converged solution reaching 99.7% of the best-known cut value. In the present paper, we elucidate the conditions under which the SSBM can be configured to emulate the behavior of a continuous-variable Ising machine. We further demonstrate that by additionally incorporating an effect analogous to physical annealing into these conditions, the SSBM becomes capable of identifying the best-known cut value for the K_2000 benchmark instance.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2609.07393v2
- Title: Mechanical quality factor estimation from a nonlinear optical cavity response
- Authors: Zohran Ali, Davide Tomasella, Daniel Allepuz-Requena, Alexander Huck, Ulrik Lund Andersen
- Categories: physics.optics (primary); physics.optics; physics.app-ph; physics.space-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07393v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.07393v2.pdf

Abstract:
Intrinsic damping rate and quality factor ($Q$) are key parameters in optomechanical systems. However, in high-Q devices, their measurement is often challenging because probe-induced dynamical backaction (DBA) and the need to resolve and accurately track the mechanical resonance limit the performance of state-of-the-art techniques. Here, we introduce an all-optical ringdown method in the unresolved-sideband regime that exploits the nonlinear, time-averaged optical response induced by strong mechanical motion. We derive an analytical model of the time-averaged optical cavity response probed by a weak frequency-scanning laser. During the evolution of the mechanical oscillator, the excited motion modulates the Lorentzian lineshape into a double-horned profile whose peak separation enables an accurate estimation of the modulation amplitude. Tracking this response during the mechanical ringdown allows the intrinsic damping rate to be determined without resolving the mechanical oscillation. We apply the method to a density-modulated phononic crystal membrane placed inside a high-finesse cavity and obtain mechanical quality factors consistent with independent measurements on the same platform. Our method requires a minimal optical setup, while offering an in situ diagnostic and reduced impact from probe-induced DBA and robustness against measurement noise. Finally, the model can describe other resonant systems dispersively coupled to coherent frequency modulations, providing new insights and measurement strategies that expand beyond the optomechanical platform.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05

- Title: Interacting quantum criticality with U(1) symmetry breaking in 1+1 dimensions
- Authors: Mutsumi Shimomura, Kohei Kawabata
- Categories: cond-mat.str-el (primary); cond-mat.str-el; cond-mat.stat-mech; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06682v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.06682v2.pdf

Abstract:
The Hohenberg-Mermin-Wagner theorem motivates the expectation that continuous symmetry remains unbroken in one-dimensional quantum ground states. Here, contrary to this expectation, we demonstrate the spontaneous breaking of U(1) spin-rotation symmetry and the emergence of an interacting quantum critical point in lattice spin-$1/2$ and spin-$1$ chains. Using subsystem Binder scaling, we locate the critical point and determine the correlation-length critical exponent as $ν= 0.60 \pm 0.02$, distinct from the Gaussian value $1/2$ but consistent with the perturbative renormalization-group value. We further estimate the dynamical critical exponent to be $z = 1.986 \pm 0.005$ from the lowest neutral excitation gap and $z = 1.955 \pm 0.021$ from the longitudinal spin correlations. The results $z < 2$ are incompatible with microscopic realizations of this critical theory by frustration-free models, implying a mechanism for continuous symmetry breaking in one-dimensional quantum ground states distinct from frustration freeness.

- Title: Norm properties of quantum uniformity measures
- Authors: Berke Ünal
- Categories: math-ph (primary); math-ph; math.FA; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17454v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17454v2.pdf

Abstract:
We give a complete classification of the norm properties of quantum uniformity measures, introduced by Bu, Gu, and Jaffe to analytically characterize the Clifford hierarchy. Resolving an open question they posed, we show that quantum uniformity measures define norms precisely for $k=2,3,4$. In particular, we establish the triangle inequality for $k=4$, whereas for every $k\geq5$ it fails in some dimension. The higher-order result is proved using the structure theory of norming systems of linear equations over finite fields. We also study the geometry of quantum uniformity measures, establishing explicit bounds on the modulus of convexity whenever they define norms. Finally, we give a complete classification of the norm properties of matrix-valued Gowers uniformity functionals, showing that only $k=2$ defines a norm.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-21 14:05
- arXiv: 2610.25878v2
- Title: Non-planar contact structures with Calabi-Yau Fillings and topological quantum computation
- Authors: Atsuhide Mori
- Categories: math.GT (primary); math.GT; math-ph; math.SG; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25878v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25878v2.pdf

Abstract:
This paper is a non-planar counterpart of the author's earlier work on planar contact structures and topological quantum computation. On a planar page every branch point is paired by a liftable arc, and the pairing matches the Ising representation exactly, in that fixing the channels of the pairs already fixes the state. Here the page has genus g and connected binding, presented as a simple degree-three cover of the disk. Every maximal system of disjoint liftable arcs then has exactly g members and leaves two branch points unused, and the half-twist interchanging the two points is not liftable, although its cube is and lifts to the identity. Ising fusion does not see that leftover pair, determining its channel from the other g and acting there only by scalars, whereas Fibonacci fusion can leave the channel undetermined. The powers of the cube then act by ten distinct relative phases on a single joint eigenspace, while the Calabi-Yau Stein filling stays fixed. An integral residue criterion supplies the Calabi-Yau test, a factorization localized on a contact connected sum is automatically Calabi-Yau, and, unlike in the planar case, a Calabi-Yau factorization need not be the shortest.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07
- arXiv: 2610.28705v1
- Title: Separability does not certify zero-noise extrapolation: Factorial diagnostics in dynamic quantum circuits
- Authors: Aryan Kumar
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28705v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28705v1.pdf

Abstract:
Separately amplifying gate, latency, and mid-circuit measurement errors can simplify zero-noise extrapolation, but a weak interaction between amplification knobs does not establish an accurate zero-noise limit. We distinguish observable separability, response-model adequacy, and calibration of that limit, and test them in a pre-specified $2^3$ factorial experiment on three IBM dynamic circuits. For the primary echoed spectator observable, the latency-measurement coefficient is $b_{LM}=+0.0007$ with a nominal 95% bootstrap interval $[-0.0033,+0.0049]$. A separately planned follow-up meets a $\pm0.005$ equivalence criterion and recovers two injected interactions; both repeat runs nevertheless trigger the drift rule. Multi-knob extrapolations have smaller median absolute errors than the single-knob comparisons, yet all five original strategies exclude the known ideal value for three of six observables. Duration controls support a model-dependent contribution to the spectator deficit from unequal decay rates in native padding and added delay. We give an explicit construction showing why measurements at two measurement-knob levels cannot identify the extrapolated limit without response assumptions. A post-hoc precision analysis and an additive full-factorial estimator expose a further bias-variance trade-off hidden by point-error comparisons. The experiment provides a diagnostic of the implemented response surface, not a certification of physical channel independence or mitigation accuracy.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07
- arXiv: 2610.28732v1
- Title: Interacting quantum reference frames
- Authors: Leonardo Chataignier, Alexander R. H. Smith
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; gr-qc; hep-th
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28732v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28732v1.pdf

Abstract:
We formulate quantum reference frames within the Dirac quantization of gauge theories, where physical states are obtained from a kinematical Hilbert space $\mathcal{H}_{\rm kin}$ by group averaging. We assume the gauge group $G$ is unimodular, and we define a quantum reference frame as a system of coherent states associated with a group $G_R$ of frame orientations acting on a tensor factor of $\mathcal{H}_{\rm kin}$. In direct analogy with the Faddeev-Popov procedure in path integrals, we show that the physical inner product can be written in terms of a choice of frame orientation with an appropriate operator measure, but that the physical Hilbert space is independent of this choice of frame. This yields a generalization of the Page-Wootters reduction map used to define conditional states and conditional probabilities of measurement outcomes given a frame orientation. These probabilities correspond to the expectation values of relational Dirac observables obtained from generalizations of previously considered relativization maps. Our main result is that the map that implements a change of frame is unitary, even when $G$ does not act as a tensor-product representation across a factorization of $\mathcal{H}_{\rm kin}$. The formalism therefore accommodates interactions between reference frames and the systems described relative to them. We apply this formalism to temporal frames, showing that a unitary Schrödinger equation emerges for an interacting clock and system, and that a previously reported time-nonlocal Schrödinger equation is recovered in an appropriate limit. With this, problems of non-unitarity are avoided by the use of the gauge-fixing measure in the physical inner product. We also discuss the case of interacting spatial frames, where the known correspondence between coherence in one frame and entanglement in another is shown to depend on the coupling.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07
- arXiv: 2610.28737v1
- Title: Local Clifford equivalence to CSS codes: Polynomial-time classification and conversion
- Authors: Mingyuan Wang, Stefan Krastanov
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28737v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28737v1.pdf

Abstract:
Calderbank--Shor--Steane (CSS) codes support decoding and fault-tolerant logical operations through separate pure $X$ and pure $Z$ stabilizer checks. Generator choices and single-qubit Clifford transformations can obscure this structure. We give a deterministic polynomial-time algorithm that decides whether an arbitrary stabilizer code is locally Clifford-equivalent to a CSS code and constructs the required transformation and CSS checks whenever such a form exists. For $m$ possibly redundant generator rows on $n$ qubits, the algorithm uses $O(mn^2+n^4)$ binary field operations. We reduce the problem to affine linear systems by characterizing local projectors that preserve the span of the binary stabilizer labels. The conversion preserves code parameters and check sparsity for quantum low-density parity-check (qLDPC) codes. We demonstrate CSS recovery and decoder reuse and resolve previously open LC-to-CSS classification questions for published code constructions. For graph states, LC-to-CSS equivalence corresponds to the existence of a bipartite graph in the local-complementation orbit. We derive a necessary-and-sufficient adjacency-matrix criterion for this property and construct such a graph whenever one exists. Our algorithm makes CSS decoding and fault-tolerant constructions accessible throughout the local Clifford equivalence classes of CSS codes.

- Title: CoDecoder: Make Quantum Decoders Up to 100x Faster and Even More Accurate
- Authors: Yicheng Guang, Yuliang Xiong, Xun Gao, Yueqi Chen
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28748v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28748v1.pdf

Abstract:
Fault-tolerant quantum computers need fast and accurate error decoding. We present CoDecoder, a small neural frontend that composes with an existing decoder to improve accuracy and reduce mean decoding time. It predicts each shot's logical outcome, retains confident predictions and routes the remaining shots to the unchanged fallback. A logical-operator-aware output head scores shifted logical operators, combining equivalent logical lines on surface codes and deciding logical qubits jointly on bivariate-bicycle (BB) codes. Fused GPU inference and efficient routing reduce the overhead paid on every shot. In registered tests under circuit-level noise, composition reduces failures by 6.4-31.4% relative to correlated matching on surface codes of distance 7-17 and by 22-50% relative to full-DEM Relay-BP on two BB codes, with 2.3-124-fold speed-ups across these comparisons. With our re-implementations of the neural decoder Cascade, composition reduces decoding time in all four settings. A joint decision of both decoders on the routed shots keeps this speed and makes the composition more accurate than Cascade in all four, with 14-18% fewer failures on the [[72,12,6]] code. On the [[72,12,6]] BB code, a conventional head with independent logical outputs fails 3.8-5.0 times as often as our joint class score under matched training conditions. Per-shot analysis shows how composition gains accuracy. On retained shots, the frontend corrects more fallback errors than it introduces, even when it is less accurate overall.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07
- arXiv: 2610.28749v1
- Title: Bias-Preserving Entanglement of Kerr-Cat Qubits with an ATS Coupler
- Authors: Kerem Yurtseven, Shantanu R. Jha, Apoorva Limaye, Risav Pokhrel, Muhammad Waqar Amin
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28749v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28749v1.pdf

Abstract:
Kerr-cat qubits offer a hardware-efficient route to fault-tolerant quantum computing by suppressing bit-flip errors exponentially, while phase-flip errors increase only linearly with the mean photon number. To maintain this protection, entangling operations must be bias-preserving, ensuring that phase-flip errors occurring during the gate do not propagate into bit-flip errors. In this paper, we propose an asymmetrically threaded SQUID (ATS) parametric coupler to implement high-fidelity, bias-preserving entangling gates between Kerr-cat qubits. By engineering a photon-number asymmetry between the control and target modes, we enable fast gate operations that suppress non-adiabatic leakage without complex pulse shaping. Through numerical simulations of the full circuit Hamiltonian, we demonstrate a bias-preserving CX gate achieving 99.69% fidelity in the absence of photon loss for a gate duration of 50 ns. Under a single-photon loss rate of 100 kHz, our protocol yields a noise bias of 9922, exceeding the noise bias of a naive non-bias-preserving CX implementation by approximately a factor of 570. These results support the ATS coupler as a valuable building block for concatenated cat-qubit architectures.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07
- arXiv: 2610.28845v1
- Title: Pushing beyond the memory barrier in propagation algorithms with randomization
- Authors: Manuel S. Rudolph, Ricard Puig, Slimane Thabet, Zoë Holmes
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; physics.comp-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28845v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28845v1.pdf

Abstract:
Performant classical tools for simulating quantum circuits are important for benchmarking and supporting upcoming quantum devices. Pauli propagation offers promising capabilities, but deep circuits usually cause a rapid growth in the number of Pauli strings and can quickly exhaust the available memory. In this work, we introduce a randomized protocol that combines a specialized resampling schedule, merging, and a controlled amount of bias to keep the propagating Pauli sum within any prescribed memory budget. Additional computational resources can then be spent on embarrassingly parallel repetitions that systematically improve the estimate. Building on the open-source library PauliPropagation.jl, we show that 1) increasing memory of each simulation reduces the error systematically more than outside averaging, 2) resampling just enough to apply the next gate delays variance accumulation and gives better error-per-time trade-offs than more aggressive resampling, and 3) a small, controlled bias is crucial in cutting down variance and pushing far beyond unbiased randomization. The resulting framework can reproduce deterministic calculations while requiring several orders of magnitude less memory.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07
- arXiv: 2610.28848v1
- Title: Tunable flat bands from a parent chiral Hamiltonian
- Authors: Jun-Hyung Bae, Alexei Andreanov, Saurabh Maiti
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.other
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28848v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28848v1.pdf

Abstract:
Systems with flat bands are interesting as they potentially host many exotic quantum phenomena that would be hard to observe in usual systems with dispersive bands. Multiple approaches were developed to construct flat band systems, with most of them requiring fine-tuning and/or solving a challenging inverse-eigenvalue problem. In this article we work with an idea that uses a parent bipartite system to generate a family of flat bands by means of Hilbert space projections. This method is largely free from fine-tuning constraints and allows us to identify conditions for a Hamiltonian to guarantee flat bands at desired energies, with desired degree of degeneracy. We also show that one can control the touching points of the flat band with dispersive bands at any desirable point in the Brillouin zone. In the process, we identify perturbations that preserve the flat bands or split them controllably. This approach provides easy tunability in designing flat band systems with desired properties.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07
- arXiv: 2610.28852v1
- Title: The Prevalence of Straight Shots in Control Space for Reaching Optimal Quantum Performance
- Authors: Yiyou Chen, Gabriele Herr, Herschel Rabitz
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; math-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28852v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28852v1.pdf

Abstract:
Gradient-based algorithms are often applied to identify an optimal control for maximizing a quantum objective functional by following a steepest ascent path to the top of the control landscape. In contrast, this paper explores straight shots through control space where movement along such a path corresponds to a less demanding monotonic complete climb up the landscape for the optimal objective value. Existing studies of control landscapes have revealed the presence of straight shots, but identifying them required computationally intensive stochastic methods. In this paper, we introduce a non-stochastic straight-shot search algorithm (STSA) that identifies a large class of straight shots. We apply STSA to numerically generate more than 10,000 distinct straight-shot optimization paths and analyze their statistical distribution for each of the objectives of (i) state-to-state transitions, (ii) general observables, and (iii) unitary transformation generation. The results show a decrease in the nearest-neighbor distances among straight shots as the sample size increases, suggesting their prevalence in control space. We further conjecture that the starting locations of the straight-shot pathways on a landscape level set may form connected neighborhoods, motivating future investigation into their underlying topological structure. The present paper focuses on the use of STSA for locating massive numbers of straight solutions. However, the STSA is not a high-efficiency algorithm in its present form, but the discovered prevalence of control straight shots leaves open future advances to accelerate the discovery of straight shots as optimal controls.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07
- arXiv: 2610.28902v1
- Title: From Qubit Tensorization to Gaussian Optimizers in Bosonic Systems
- Authors: Salman Beigi, Hami Mehrabi
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28902v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28902v1.pdf

Abstract:
We develop a method for deriving Gaussian optimality results for bosonic quantum systems from inequalities for qubit systems. Inspired by Gross's derivation of the Gaussian logarithmic Sobolev inequality from an inequality on the two-point space, we use qubits as quantum two-point spaces and lift bosonic Weyl operators to pseudo-Weyl operators on large qubit systems. By controlling weighted norms and relative entropy in the fluctuation limit, we establish two transfer theorems that convert tensorized qubit inequalities into inequalities for bosonic Gaussian channels. As a first application, we prove that the sharp $2$-logarithmic Sobolev inequality for the bosonic Ornstein--Uhlenbeck semigroup tensorizes without loss in the constant. We also establish a sharp output entropy inequality for tensor powers of a class of qubit channels. This includes a quantum Mrs.~Gerber's lemma determining the minimum output entropy of tensor powers of the qubit depolarizing channel at fixed input entropy. Applying our relative entropy transfer theorem, we obtain multimode constrained minimum output entropy bounds for Gaussian additive-noise channels and thermal attenuators in the heating range.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07
- arXiv: 2610.28964v1
- Title: Can Quantum Layers offer Performance and Security Advantages in Vision-Language Models?
- Authors: Md Khalid Syfullah, Alvi Ataur Khalil
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.AI; cs.CV; cs.LG
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28964v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28964v1.pdf

Abstract:
As quantum computing moves from the noisy intermediate-scale quantum (NISQ) era toward early fault-tolerant quantum computing (EFTQC), quantum-enhanced AI is shifting from a conceptual idea to experimental research. Quantum components are expected to extend the capabilities of existing AI systems, yet it remains unclear how such components will help within large multimodal models. In this study, we try to answer that question for vision-language models (VLMs) using classically simulated quantum layers. With LLaVA-OneVision as the backbone, we evaluate 4- and 8-qubit basic-ring and strongly entangling circuits at four insertion and four replacement locations. Insertion adds quantum layers to the classical model, while replacement substitutes them for classical components. Using the same setup, we compare performance and computational cost between classical and quantum-classical hybrid models. On the Visual Spatial Reasoning dataset, inserting a quantum layer after the visual projector reaches 80.7% development accuracy, 2.28 percentage-points above classical full fine-tuning. Replacing the final decoder feed-forward sublayer retains 79.22% accuracy with 13.07 million fewer trainable parameters. Quantum insertion can thus improve accuracy, and selective replacement can reduce parameters. These benefits depend strongly on where the quantum layer is inserted or which classical component it replaces: earlier replacements lower accuracy, and fewer parameters do not reliably shorten training or reduce computation. To assess potential security benefits, we evaluated the robustness of quantum-classical hybrid models under six attack settings. Overall, our study maps where quantum layers help and hurt within VLMs, offering a starting point for hybrid VLM design; whether these gains generalize remains open. The code is available at https://github.com/TITANS-Lab-Artifacts/QuantumVLM.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07
- arXiv: 2610.28993v1
- Title: Digital Quantum Simulation of Dynamics in Interacting Fields
- Authors: Zhiyao Li
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; hep-lat; nucl-th
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28993v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28993v1.pdf

Abstract:
Matter under extreme conditions behaves as a non-equilibrium, strongly-interacting quantum system. Such systems are in the non-perturbative regime of quantum chromodynamics (QCD), where their time evolution is classically intractable. Quantum computing offers a promising alternative, but present-day devices are limited in qubit count and circuit depth, so efficient formulations and algorithms are key to scaling toward experimentally relevant QCD simulations. A scalable framework is established for simulating energy loss and hadronization in 1+1-dimensional SU(2) lattice gauge theory, in which a heavy quark traverses a lattice of light quarks. Classical lattice QCD methods are translated into physics-informed circuits to prepare the interacting ground state. The evolution is run on IBM's quantum processor ibm_pittsburgh, where the charge distribution and energy loss are measured. A further study develops the quantum simulation of the fully gauge-fixed SU(2) Hamiltonian in a mixed basis, a formulation that is expected to remain efficient at all gauge couplings. Digitizing the continuous gauge fields with as few as three qubits per plaquette reproduces low-energy observables with $10^{-3}$ precision. Scaling to larger systems is limited by resource overhead, which motivates truncations that preserve the relevant observables. Sequency Hierarchy Truncation (SeqHT) exploits the hierarchy in basis-operator contributions. Demonstrated on the adiabatic preparation of the $λφ^4$ ground state on ibm_sherbrooke, SeqHT reduces circuit depth by ~30%, suppressing hardware noise in the extracted observables. All simulations in this dissertation are validated on quantum hardware against classical benchmarks. These studies advance quantum simulations of real-time QCD dynamics.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07
- arXiv: 2610.29035v1
- Title: Optimization versus fair sampling in quantum annealing: An exactly characterized frustrated Ising benchmark
- Authors: Masaru Hitomi, Masayuki Ohzeki
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29035v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29035v1.pdf

Abstract:
Reaching a degenerate ground-state manifold and sampling it uniformly are distinct properties of a quantum annealer. We quantify this distinction using an Ising ring with additional couplings between maximally separated sites. For antiferromagnetic benchmark couplings and $N \geq 5$, we derive the exact ground-state energy, degeneracy, and defect structure for each congruence class of $N$ modulo four. This classification separates ground-state hit probability from conditional sampling bias. We embed instances with up to 162 logical spins on a D-Wave Advantage processor using length-two chains and compare its output with simulated annealing on the embedded and logical problems. For a frustrated $N=83$ instance, the processor visits 164 of the 166 exact ground states, yet the conditional distributions over states and defect sectors remain nonuniform. For $N=160$, the processor has a larger ground-state hit probability than logical simulated annealing (0.869 versus 0.519), but also a larger total-variation distance from uniformity (0.317 versus 0.136). A pause during the anneal raises the ground-state hit probability from 0.42 to 0.86 without restoring uniform sampling. These results identify a relaxation window and demonstrate that optimization success and sampling quality need not improve together, providing an exactly characterized benchmark for open-system quantum annealing.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07

- Title: Buffer-Qubit-Based Quantum Circuit Allocation Method Against Crosstalk Attacks
- Authors: Haruki Nakayama, Satoyuki Tsukano, Satoshi Kono, Keita Matsumoto, Toshio Mori, Takumi Oikawa, Ryo Uchida
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29050v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29050v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum Multi-Programming (QMP) enables the simultaneous execution of quantum circuits from multiple users on a single quantum chip in cloud quantum computing services. However, QMP is vulnerable to crosstalk attacks, in which a malicious user deliberately induces crosstalk to disrupt other users' computations. As a countermeasure, prior work has proposed the use of buffer qubits, which remain idle and are allocated between quantum circuits to suppress the effect of crosstalk. Nevertheless, to the best of our knowledge, such countermeasures have not been publicly reported in production quantum cloud software. In this paper, we extend the QMP of OQTOPUS, open-source software that is already in production use for operating real quantum computers, with a buffer-qubit-based circuit allocation method. Experiments on a real device characterize the attack effect in terms of energy relaxation and dephasing, showing that observable degradation appears in dephasing characteristics of qubits adjacent to the attacker circuit. Based on this result, we release the proposed method as part of OQTOPUS and confirm that the buffer qubits are correctly reserved around the allocated circuits.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07
- arXiv: 2610.29058v1
- Title: QUBO-Based Conformer Selection with Quantum-Classical Hybrid Optimization for Multiconformer Ligand Modeling
- Authors: Jiyun Lee, Haeseong Kim, Junho Lee, Joonsuk Huh
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; q-bio.BM
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29058v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29058v1.pdf

Abstract:
A ligand can adopt multiple conformations within the same binding site, and these conformations can interact differently with the surrounding protein. Multiconformer modeling must therefore determine which candidate conformations to retain and what occupancies to assign to them. An established automated approach uses mixed-integer quadratic programming (MIQP) with hard occupancy and conformer-count limits for this selection. Here, we reformulated conformer selection as a quadratic unconstrained binary optimization (QUBO) problem with finite penalties and discrete occupancies. We solved the QUBO using a quantum-classical hybrid solver. We compared this workflow with the MIQP-based qFit-ligand workflow across 83 paired X-ray ligand entries using the same experimental inputs and the same qFit-ligand processing protocol. For each entry, both final models were evaluated against the same composite-omit map generated from the common input structure before the two workflows diverged. Quantum-qFit produced higher ligand-region real-space correlation coefficients (RSCC) in 62 entries 74.7% and lower occupancy-weighted torsional strain in 66 entries 79.5%. Similar directional trends remained in the 28 entries with matched final conformer counts. The molecular Electron Density for Individual Atoms score (EDIAm) decreased in most entries. This result shows that higher regional map agreement did not necessarily correspond to stronger atom-level density support. Overall, the results show that the conformer-selection formulation can change which ligand states are represented from the same experimental data and provide a QUBO framework for studying multiconformer ligand selection.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07

- Title: Fast Template Matching for Quantum Circuits Using Hypergraph
- Authors: Xin Hong, Jintao Yu, Runhong He, Shenggang Ying, Sanjiang Li, Mingsheng Ying
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29095v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29095v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum circuit optimization is a key step toward the efficient execution of quantum algorithms. Template matching has emerged as one of the dominant approaches for simplifying quantum circuits, yet it confronts the intrinsic challenge of accommodating gate commutativity. The state-of-the-art template-matching algorithm relies on a directed acyclic graph (DAG) representation. While this DAG-based technique handles gate commutativity satisfactorily, it fails to preserve the local connectivity inherent to quantum circuits, thereby leading to relatively high matching complexity.   In this paper, we introduce a hypergraph representation (HG), a commutativity-aware representation that collapses commuting gates into a single super-node while retaining all local connectivity. This enables matches to be extended locally without revisiting the rest of the circuit, with incremental updates limited to the immediate neighborhood. Experimental results demonstrate that our HG matcher achieves 11--606x speed-up over the DAG-based implementation in Qiskit (Iten et al., 2022) on both random and arithmetic benchmarks, while maintaining the same optimization quality. The acceleration increases with circuit size, confirming that preserving locality and connectivity is the key to scalable quantum circuit optimisation.

Abstract:
Quantum Fisher information (QFI) is a fundamental quantity in quantum information and quantum mechanics, and evaluating it in various physical systems has become an important research topic. However, its evaluation in large systems still faces many challenges. Hereby we propose an ancilla-based protocol for evaluating the QFI of large systems and establish the conditions under which this protocol can ``perfectly'' evaluate the QFI in theory. - Date (JST ingest): 2026-10-22 14:07
- arXiv: 2610.29170v1
- Title: Turning Quantum Compiler Configuration Selection Upside Down: A Top-Down Paradigm
- Authors: Ming Zhong, Xiangyu Ren, Kangyu Zheng, Zhi Zeng, Shenggen Zheng, Shaohua Li, Zhiding Liang
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.SE
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29170v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29170v1.pdf

Abstract:
Selecting an effective quantum compiler configuration requires coordinated decisions over both hardware targets and compiler settings, with the ultimate goal of improving circuit execution fidelity. Existing SOTA approaches typically decompose this problem into two sequential stages: a device is chosen first, after which compiler options are tuned on that device through RL-based search. This bottom-up strategy can be fundamentally limited by its initial device decision, since a poorly matched backend may constrain the best fidelity achievable regardless of how effectively later compiler options are optimized. In contrast to classical compiler tuning, where the search space is often prohibitively large, the configuration space of quantum compilers remains manageable enough to support exhaustive search. Consequently, rather than repeatedly searching from candidate configurations, one can derive reusable configuration knowledge from offline oracle results and apply it directly to new circuits.   In this paper, we introduce a top-down configuration-selection paradigm and build QuOPTBench, an exhaustively searched benchmark containing oracle configurations for 268 quantum circuits. We first study whether LLMs can directly infer suitable configurations and evaluate ten representative LLMs. The results reveal that vanilla LLMs remain far from the oracle. We then employ an LLM to extract transferable rules from sampled circuits governing configuration selection. Using these rules together with predictors trained on the enumerated configuration data of the sampled circuits, we design QuTOP, a rule-guided ML framework that performs fast configuration selection without iterative online search. Across our evaluation, the distilled rules consistently strengthen LLM-based selection, while QuTOP provides the strongest overall balance between solution quality and selection latency among the baselines.

- Title: Readout Dependent Parameter Identifiability of Quantum Kernels
- Authors: Hyong Keun Park, Jahoon Jeong, Joonkyo Shim, Hyunsoo Yoon
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29186v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29186v1.pdf

Abstract:
A circuit parameter can change measured features without changing their projected Gaussian kernel. We characterize this lost dependence and distinguish recovery by metric design from nonlinear feature expansion. With analytic feature coefficients, a fixed finite data basis affinely independent on the input domain, a fixed positive definite metric, and fixed bandwidth, the coefficient Gram matrix determines all-input kernel equivalence, global coordinate inactivity, and generic differential rank. An exact four-qubit family exhibits a sharp boundary: diagonal metrics give rank two, dense metrics reach but cannot exceed three, and no fixed affine expansion crosses this ceiling. One appended nonlinear scalar coordinate suffices for rank four and is minimal when the original features are retained. A single pairwise product achieves this with an explicit nondiagonal metric; a scalar sum of two products achieves it with a diagonal metric. Both use existing ideal local means. Exact certificates establish ranks in 54 selected circuit/readout settings. Matched finite-shot comparisons show that minimal representation size need not give the best sensitivity among the tested designs. Under a factorized measurement distribution, conditional product estimation cannot increase variance at a fixed design. Execution audits further separate kernel identifiability from compiled cost.

- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29190v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29190v1.pdf

Abstract:
The solution of combinatorial optimization problems by means of quantum annealing is based on the preparation of an equally superposition state. Such superposition is crucial to the associative memory problem of correctly recalling patterns. In this paper, we study the alignment of the amplitudes of stored states in memory using the proposed auxiliary Hamiltonian, which is switched off at the end of evolution. The results of the algorithm execution were obtained both by numerical modeling and by the perturbation theory method, which made it possible to obtain the dependences on physical parameters in explicit form. As examples, systems of two and three qutrits, represented by spins S=1, are considered. Since the Hamiltonian is expressed in a univer-sal form through projectors onto states stored in memory, it can be used for other quantum sys-tems (platforms).

- Title: PQ-Vault: Provably Quantum-Safe Asset Custody for Unmodified Ethereum
- Authors: Hassan Khodaiemehr, Khadijeh Bagheri, Chen Feng
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.CR; cs.DC; cs.IT
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29230v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29230v1.pdf

Abstract:
A quantum computer could recover the key of any Ethereum account whose public key appears on chain. We prove that no sidechain or bridge that leaves Ethereum's rules unchanged can protect such accounts: their funds must move into a contract whose release condition relies only on quantum-safe primitives. We design \emph{PQ-Vault}, a custody system for unmodified Ethereum combining this vault, a quantum-safe proof-of-stake sidechain whose checkpoints carry compact certificates, and an optimistic dispute protocol. We give the first security proof of compact certificates against quantum attackers and a matching attack: with classically chosen parameters, the guarantee falls from 128 to about 60 bits, and a simple rule restores it. We show that a common seed-update rule in hash-chain proof of stake lets an attacker steer chosen slots and profit from splitting its stake, and give an election whose steering gain and inclusion delay are provably bounded against quantum attackers. Since compressing certificates with a pairing-based SNARK breaks quantum security, we use fraud proofs that need a single signature check. For 65,536 validators, a certificate is 0.8 MB, 260 times smaller than all signatures, and verifies in about 20 ms; the vault accepts a checkpoint for about 420,000 gas.
